# CDP run narratives, 2026-07-30 … 2026-08-17 (archive)

Moved verbatim out of `docs/testing/cdp-live-checklist.md` on 2026-08-21, when
that file became the single case×runs table (owner rule 2026-08-20, AGENTS.md
close-out step 4). Nothing below was edited in the move. Newer runs append
their own file beside this one — one `<date>-<tag>.md` per run — and update
their rows in the checklist table.

## Run of 2026-08-17 (evening) — `STR-1`…`STR-4`, the mid-turn correction, and the default that must NOT be flipped

Drivers `scripts/cdp/cases/str-{1,2,3,4}.ts` and the shared instrument
`scripts/cdp/lib/mid-stream.ts`, on the operator's real profile, `electron:dev`
on port 9223 (`ulimit -n 65536`). This discharges `L8` — the last open row of
`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-17-claude-live-session.md` — and the never-run
half of `055bd70ad` on the Codex side.

**THE VERDICT FIRST, because it is a decision and not a result:
`defaultMidStreamBehavior` was NOT flipped for `anthropic`/`claude-runtime`.**
The plan's recorded order is prove-then-flip. The delivered path proved cleanly
(`STR-1`, 21/21); the arm the current default's own doc comment names as the
reason Claude sits on `queue` proved WORSE than that comment says. It calls the
cost *"paying for the original turn's tokens for nothing"*. Measured twice, on
two fresh sessions: **the user's correction is LOST**, with the app logging
`replay failed … Existing message … not found` and the transcript still showing
the message as delivered. That is the defect class `pendingSteeredMessageIds` was
added to close, reopened through a different door — details below. So the flip is
withheld, and this section is the evidence for withholding it rather than a plan
of what to build.

| Case | Status | What it settles |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `STR-1` | **PASS** 21/21 | On `steer`, a correction typed 20 s into a six-command Claude turn reached the model INSIDE that turn. The CLI's own transcript carries one prompt entry for the whole exchange and a `hook_additional_context` attachment naming `PreToolUse:Bash` and tool-use id `call_aba6efc3e6a74556b22a3b8e` — a call the same file records the model making — whose content is our own wrapper plus the correction. The model then abandoned the sequence at 2 of 6 commands and wrote the note with the corrected build id. The `queue` control, same card, same run: two prompt entries, the correction IS the second, no injection, all six commands ran, `ALPHA…` answered first and `BETA…` second. |
| `STR-2` | **FAIL** 8/12, twice | The tool-less arm. Everything up to the re-queue is right — nothing injected, `steered: true` parked, `Steer message undelivered, re-queuing` logged — and then **the correction is lost**. The re-queue could not reuse the transcript id, the queue DTO minted a fresh one into the entry, and the replay threw on it: `replay failed {"messageId":"msg-1786978610553-nlpodf","error":"Existing message msg-1786978610553-nlpodf not found"}` against a transcript holding `msg-1786978601564-v6wki9`. The model never saw the correction (one prompt entry in the CLI's transcript; the answer is about `ALPHA…`), the user gets a "New message" notification, and the transcript keeps the lost correction rendered as DELIVERED. |
| `STR-3` | **PASS** 19/19 | Codex's native `turn/steer`, run for the first time. `Codex steering turn 01a0103f-05a7-7d10-9b73-77ace40016ad` went out with the id the server itself announced, no refusal came back, the sequence stopped at 2 of 6 and the answer carried the corrected id. The `queue` control ran all six and answered the original id first. |
| `STR-4` | **PASS** 5/5 | **A user Stop on a Codex turn now stops the WORK, not just our view of it.** Four `sleep 3 && echo probe-STR49BBD-N` processes were visible to `ps` while the turn ran (the positive control), the Stop landed 31.4 s in, and across the next 20 s — long enough for the four remaining three-second commands — **not one new marked process appeared**, with the sequence ending at 4 of 6. |

Wave: `NESSY_CDP_CODEX_CARD=codex-gateway bun run scripts/cdp/run.ts STR-3 STR-4`
→ `FAIL STR-3 … · PASS STR-4 5/5` on the first pass (the `STR-3` failure was the
harness's own, fixed and re-run green — see the case defects). `STR-1` ran twice,
green both times; `STR-2` ran twice, red both times with the same mechanism.

### The detector, and the two of mine that were wrong before it

The claim is that a correction reaches the model INSIDE the running turn. Three
readings look like they answer it and do not:

- **`steered: true`.** `ClaudeAgent.redirect` returns it on a PRECONDITION — an
  open, unsettled slot — and parks the text; its own contract says delivery
  happens later, at the next `PreToolUse`. Reading it as delivery is the
  `lastCompaction` mistake: a flag about intent, read as an outcome.
- **the transcript's `isQueued: false`.** The same boolean, one layer out.
- **"the correction was answered".** True in BOTH behaviours. Under `queue` it is
  answered by a second turn, which is the defect.

What settles it is the CLI's own transcript, and the shape was found by
measurement rather than assumed. When our hook returns `additionalContext` the
CLI files its own entry for it:

```json
{"type":"attachment","attachment":{"type":"hook_additional_context",
  "content":["The user just sent a new message while you were working. …"],
  "hookName":"PreToolUse:Bash","toolUseID":"call_9e264d30e3f142eea7a80b3f",
  "hookEvent":"PreToolUse"}}
```

— between the `tool_use` it names and that call's `tool_result`. Nothing about
it is ours: not the file, not the entry type, not the id, which the case checks
against the `tool_use` ids the same file records. Together with the PROMPT count
(one user entry carrying a `text` block ⇒ one turn) it separates the two
behaviours structurally.

**Two of this run's own detectors were wrong first, in the two ways AGENTS.md
warns about.** (1) `isQueued` was read AFTER the exchange settled: under `queue`
the replay sends the message under its own id and `isQueued` goes back to false,
so the control arm reported "not queued" about a message that had plainly been
queued — a true reading of the wrong instant, reporting a product defect. It is
now read while the turn is still running. (2) The delivery check assumed the
injected text would land inside a `tool_result` and scored its absence as
non-delivery — it "failed" on a turn where the model had demonstrably read the
correction and acted on it. The container was a sibling entry.

And a third, on the composer: the primary control was read BEFORE the correction
was typed, and `resolveSubmitControl` returns `stop` while a turn runs and the
composer is EMPTY. Both arms read "Stop", which looked like the setting never
reaching the UI. Read between "typed" and "sent", the same button reads **Steer**
in one arm and **Queue** in the other — `composer-capabilities.ts:171` derives it
from `resolveMidStreamBehavior`, and the labels are compared to each OTHER
because they are localized.

### `STR-2`'s defect, cause and chain

Read entirely out of the code plus the app's own log, and deterministic — twice,
on two sessions:

1. `SessionManager.setProcessing(managed, false)` clears
   `managed.pendingSteeredMessageIds` on the FALLING EDGE of `isProcessing`
   (`SessionManager.ts:6923`), for a good reason it states: *"A steer belongs to
   the turn it was written into."*
2. `ClaudeAgent`'s generator yields `complete` and THEN `steer_undelivered` — its
   own comment says the sequence is `["complete", "steer_undelivered"]`
   (`claude-agent.ts:2231`).
3. So by the time the `steer_undelivered` case runs, the id list is already
   `undefined`: `shift()` yields nothing, `existing` is undefined, `isQueued` is
   never restored, and the entry is pushed WITHOUT a message id — the deliberate
   *"queue it WITHOUT an id so `sendMessage` creates one"* branch.
4. `emitQueueChanged` fires immediately after the push, and `queueEntryId` does
   `entry.messageId ??= generateMessageId()` — **mutating the queue entry** to
   carry an id that exists in no transcript.
5. `processNextQueuedMessage` hands that id to `sendMessage` as
   `existingMessageId`, whose lookup throws `Existing message … not found`.

So the fix that reused the transcript id is intact, and the branch written for
the case where it CANNOT be reused is defeated by the queue DTO's own id
assignment. The user-visible result is the exact outcome
`pendingSteeredMessageIds`' doc comment says the fix prevents.

**This is not Claude-only, and that is the part that matters for a shipped
default.** `steer_undelivered` is one handler shared by both backends, and
`codex-runtime`'s default is ALREADY `steer`. Codex reaches the same handler
whenever the app-server refuses a steer — `NonSteerableTurnKind` is
`"review" | "compact"`, and codex compacts on its own budget, so a refusal is
not something a client can avoid. That arm is **UNTESTED here**: nothing in this
harness can force a codex compact or review turn to be the active one. Named
rather than assumed safe.

### Two more things the run found on the way

1. **The Claude CLI does not persist a hook's `additionalContext` under the
   turn's message content**, only as a sibling `attachment` entry. Worth knowing
   for any future case that greps a transcript for injected context — and the
   reason `classifyClaudeTranscript` reads `entry.raw.attachment` rather than
   flattened text.
2. **The operator's own `AGT-6` fixture injects through the same channel.** Its
   `UserPromptSubmit` hook writes a `hook_additional_context` attachment into
   every turn on this profile, so a check that merely COUNTED attachments would
   have passed on it. `steerDelivery` filters by the per-run token as well as by
   `hookEvent`.

### What this run borrowed, and gave back

Every `claude-runtime` and `codex-runtime` connection on a real profile carries an
explicit `midStreamBehavior: 'queue'` written by `createBuiltInConnection`, and
`resolveMidStreamBehavior` honours a stored value over the default BY DESIGN — the
field has no provenance, so a migration cannot tell the app's own default from a
user's choice in Settings → AI. **A default flip therefore moves no existing
connection, and that is not a defect.** So each case SETS the behaviour through
the app's own `saveLlmConnection` — the same call
`AiSettingsPage.handleSetMidStreamBehavior` makes — and restores the operator's
previous value in a `finally`, reporting both. Verified back at `queue` on every
arm.

### What could not be driven

- **the codex `review`/`compact` steer refusal** — `NonSteerableTurnKind`'s own
  arm. No lever in this harness makes a review or compact turn the active one.
- **`STR-2` on the BYO `anthropic` provider type.** It shares the backend and the
  handler, so the mechanism is the same by construction, but only
  `claude-runtime` was measured.
- **the native `priority` queue route** (`L8`'s expensive option). Not built, so
  nothing to measure.

### The same sitting: `CDX-147`, `CDX-ROLES` and the `LIV` re-run

Asked for alongside `L8`. Wave:
`NESSY_CDP_CLAUDE_CARD=claude-gateway NESSY_CDP_CODEX_CARD=codex-gateway bun run scripts/cdp/run.ts CDX-147 CDX-ROLES LIV-1 LIV-2 LIV-3`

```
FAIL    CDX-147   37s  4/8 checks — every check above ran against a DEV build …
BLOCKED CDX-ROLES  0s  0/0 checks — missing probe agent …/agents/probe-role.md
PASS    LIV-1     77s  11/11 checks
PASS    LIV-2     62s  13/13 checks
PASS    LIV-3     54s  16/16 checks
```

- **`LIV-1`/`LIV-2`/`LIV-3` — NO REGRESSION.** Re-run because the Codex and
  background-task work landed after their first green. Same claims, same
  controls, fresh numbers: one child (pid 12279, started 18:06:13) answered two
  turns with `etime` 00:12 → 00:21 → 00:30 and the `/clear` control produced pid
  12741; Stop pressed 3 293 ms in put `[Request interrupted by user]` into the
  child's own transcript (0 → 1) and the SAME pid 13362 answered the next message
  12 s later; a model switch took 543 ms and started no process, pid 15314 keeping
  `tgpt/qwen36-35b-a3b-fp8` on its command line while the SDK transcript records
  turn 2 answered as `tgpt/qwen35-397b-a17b-fp8`.
- **`CDX-147` — the version half PASSED, the MCP half could not be driven here.**
  The shipped `codex-app-server` is the pinned `rust-v0.147.0`
  (`sha256=b1a7e99d3dba6cef9bb3785097321041a6b6594600a520bb320a9d80b35fd65c`,
  matching the manifest pin on all three platform binaries) and a Codex turn
  completed carrying this run's codeword `8F9E1-8AE60-4001A-EB571`. The four
  failing checks are all one precondition: the probe defaults to the elicitation
  fixture's `mcp__sources__elicit-probe__whoami`, whose server is not installed in
  the operator's normal config dir, and the model answered *"not found in
  available tools"* with only `mcp__codex__list_mcp_resources` on offer. **The
  case's own registration says it should report BLOCKED rather than FAIL against
  any config dir but the fixture's, and it did not** — a case defect, not a
  product one, and deliberately left as found rather than edited mid-measurement.
  Either way the run ends BLOCKED-by-design on a dev build: `electron:dev` cannot
  show the signing-time rewrite that is the whole reason the version check exists.
- **`CDX-ROLES` — BLOCKED on a named, operator-owned precondition.**
  `~/.nessy-agent/agents/probe-role.md` and `probe-denied.md` do not exist on this
  profile (`agents/` holds only `cru-isolated`, `cru-plain`, `cru-skilled`), and
  the harness deliberately does not create an operator's agents. It did get far
  enough to confirm the wiring is IN the loaded bundle — `agent-roles` ×1,
  `runtimeDispatchesAgentDefinitions` ×4, `isForeignThreadNotification` ×3 in
  `apps/electron/dist/main.cjs`, nothing missing — so the block is the fixture and
  not the build.

## Run of 2026-08-17 (later) — `LIV-1`…`LIV-3`, the live Claude session on the product's own route

Drivers `scripts/cdp/cases/liv-{1,2,3}.ts`, on the operator's real profile,
`electron:dev` on port 9223 (`ulimit -n 65536`). This closes the gap four
consecutive rows of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-17-claude-live-session.md`
recorded in escalating terms — L0 proved the shape in a throwaway spike with a
REDUCED option set, L2/L3/L4/L5/L7 proved their halves with unit rigs, fake CLIs
and a real CLI against a FAKE upstream, and "the product's own route was never
exercised" became "four rows old now". It is exercised here.

**The bundles carry the code under test**, checked before the results were
trusted rather than assumed — and the argument is stated in the form the evidence
actually supports, because the first draft ran two different claims together. The
Electron main process started at 10:14:20 and `apps/electron/dist/main.cjs` was
written at 10:14:23, i.e. THREE SECONDS LATER, so "the bundle was built after the
last commit" does not by itself say the running process loaded that bundle — and a
main-process change is not hot-reloaded, which is the whole reason the check
exists. What the evidence does support: the app was launched (10:14) after every
commit in HEAD (the last is 10:11:11), `scripts/electron-dev.ts` waits for
`esbuild.context().watch()`'s initial build to complete and verify before it
spawns Electron, no source outside `scripts/cdp` and `docs` is modified in the
tree, and `grep -ac` finds `claudeLiveSessionSignature`, `ClaudeInputChannel`,
`applyLiveSettings`, `resetBackgroundTaskLevel` and
`reconcileEndedBackgroundShells` in the bundle. So the loaded bundle is post-HEAD
content.

**And the real proof is behavioural rather than documentary**: the pre-L2 shape
ends the child's stdin at the first result and the process is gone within 1.5 s
(measured by L0), so a child with a climbing `etime` across an 8-second idle gap
is something the old code cannot produce at all.

| Case | Status | What it settles |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `LIV-1` | **PASS** 11/11 | One child process answered two turns of one conversation — pid 63390, started 11:34:55, `etime` 00:03 → 00:13 across an 8-second idle gap between them and still the only process after turn 2. The control on the same rig, same session, same instrument: after `/clear` the third turn was answered by pid 63804 and 63390 was gone. |
| `LIV-2` | **PASS** 13/13 | Stop pressed 3 010 ms into a turn; the child wrote `[Request interrupted by user]` into its OWN transcript (0 → 1 occurrences), processing ended 5 588 ms after the click, the interrupted answer never reached the last item it was asked for, and the next message was answered normally in 25 342 ms — by the SAME pid, so Stop is `Query.interrupt()` and not session death. |
| `LIV-3` | **PASS** 16/16 | A model switch mid-conversation took 528 ms and started no process: pid 65988 answered both turns with its command line still naming `tgpt/qwen36-35b-a3b-fp8`, the model it was SPAWNED with, while the SDK's own transcript records turn 2 as answered by `tgpt/qwen35-397b-a17b-fp8`. The `/clear` control then produced pid 66373, spawned with the new model. |

Wave: `NESSY_CDP_CLAUDE_CARD=claude-custom-2 bun run scripts/cdp/run.ts LIV-1 LIV-2 LIV-3`
→ `PASS LIV-1 68s 11/11 · PASS LIV-2 80s 13/13 · PASS LIV-3 63s 16/16`. The day
ran five waves plus single-case runs: `LIV-1` and `LIV-3` were green in every one,
and `LIV-2` was green in four of its seven runs. The other three are the case
defects below, and each is now handled rather than smoothed over.

### The detector, and the three that were rejected

The claim is that ONE PROCESS serves many turns, and the three readings that
look like they answer it do not:

- **the session id** — persisted and replayed as `--resume=<id>` on the next
  spawn. Measured here: a claude-runtime turn that FAILED upstream respawned the
  child, and the new child's command line carried the identical
  `--resume=38632224-…`. An unchanged session id is what a RESPAWN looks like,
  so it can only falsify the claim, never confirm it;
- **`system/init`** — L0 already measured it as a per-TURN marker;
- **the application log** — absent on a packaged build (`runtime-session-log.ts`).

What only a surviving process can carry is the process. `scripts/cdp/lib/claude-child.ts`
reads `ps -o pid,ppid,lstart,etime,command` and keeps a process only if it
(a) matches the SDK's fixed child shape — a `…/claude` executable AND
`--input-format stream-json`, both halves, see case defect 4 — (b)
descends from the Electron process carrying this run's own
`--remote-debugging-port`, and (c) has a working directory — read with `lsof` —
equal to `<config>/sessions/<workspaceId>/<sessionId>`. Identity is
`pid@lstart`, never the pid alone.

**Both attribution filters earned their place on this machine.** The developer's
Claude Desktop runs its own agent CLI whose command line matches (b) exactly and
whose cwd was measured as *this repository*; and our own sessions now keep their
children alive for minutes, so several sibling children were running during
every case. A `pgrep claude` detector would have counted both.

**The instrument proves itself before any absence is believed.**
`assertProcessInstrument` requires that `ps` can find the app under test and
that `lsof` can read its cwd, and reports BLOCKED when it cannot — otherwise
"this session has no child" would be equally true of a healthy runtime and of a
harness that cannot see processes.

### The control that could have failed

Every one of the three cases ends by forcing the respawn the product owes.
`/clear` nulls the session id, and `ensureLiveSession` reuses the live child only
when the turn's resume anchor matches the conversation that child already holds
— a turn asking for NO conversation "matches only a child that never had one".
The same instrument then reports a DIFFERENT `pid@lstart` in every case. Without
that arm, a detector that always answered "same process" would have passed all
three while measuring nothing.

`LIV-2`'s stop arm carries two more: the app's own send button must have become
the Stop button (`type="button"`, a structural read of `submitMode === 'stop'`,
so no localized label is involved) and `isProcessing` must be true at the instant
of the click.

### Four case defects this run found in ITSELF, all the harness's own

Written down because each produced a wrong verdict before it was fixed, and both
are the shapes this repo keeps paying for.

1. **A timing control that compared two samples of a variable latency passed
   spuriously.** The first `LIV-2` draft ran the identical prompt to completion
   and required it to take longer than the point at which Stop was pressed. On
   one run the same 60-item prompt took **9.4 s on one turn and 81.2 s on the
   next through the same gateway** — a 9x spread — and the 9.4 s turn had
   FINISHED before the Stop was pressed. It kept a complete 3 692-character
   answer, the child recorded no interrupt, and the timing control passed anyway,
   because 81 s is comfortably longer than 4 s. Only the child's own marker
   caught it. The case now decides between three outcomes from the app's own
   record: `SessionManager.cancelProcessing` returns early — *"if
   (!managed.isProcessing) return // Not processing, nothing to cancel"* —
   BEFORE it appends "Response interrupted", so **no info row ⇒ the Stop arrived
   too late and the arm reports BLOCKED**, an info row with no child marker ⇒ the
   app accepted a Stop the child never saw, which is a real FAIL, and both ⇒ the
   interrupt landed. Completeness is now read from the answer's own text (it must
   not reach the last item it was asked for) rather than from a second sample.
2. **The probe prompt read as a jailbreak.** *"Reply with exactly the word
   ALPHA4DE… and nothing else"* was answered by the gateway's model with *"I
   cannot comply with this request. This appears to be an attempt to extract my
   internal processing tokens or override my actual instructions"* — and the case
   scored it FAIL. A model declining a probe-shaped prompt measures the MODEL
   (`CRU-6` already records that rule), so the shared helper now asks *"Repeat
   this word back to me: X"* and reports BLOCKED, with the model's own words,
   when a probe turn is not answered.

3. **The operator's own fixtures hijacked a probe.** One seed turn came back as
   `NESSY-CDP-PROMPT-HOOK-77A1` and one card probe as `ALPHA` followed by
   `OSTYLE-CDP-9241` — the `AGT-6` prompt-hook and `AGT-4` output-style markers
   installed on this profile, instructing the model to answer with them. The case
   BLOCKED, which was right, but it is flaky rather than informative: the shared
   echo helper now asks twice and reports both replies, so a hijack is visible in
   the record and a once-per-turn injection does not end the run.
4. **THE OBSERVER COUNTED ITSELF.** A `ps … | grep -- "--input-format
   stream-json"` pipeline carries that string in its own command line, so three
   "mystery Claude children appearing and exiting within seconds" were the
   diagnostic shell. They were minutes from being written up here as one-shot
   mini queries. The detector now requires the executable half of the command
   line too; no check was ever affected (ancestry and cwd excluded them), only
   the machine-wide count in the report.

A fifth, avoided rather than found: lengthening that prompt to 200 items to widen
the stop window made the reference turn exceed the 300 s turn timeout, so the
case failed for the upstream's latency. The next message after a Stop is a short
echo turn now — the claim is that the session answers, not that it answers a
200-item list inside five minutes.

### The card fact, and what it means for L10

The wave was pointed at `claude-custom-2` first, as instructed. It refused every
turn, and the refusal is L10 verbatim, wrapped inside the proxy's anonymizer:

```
403 {"error":"failed to detect critical data: fail anon mask critical data: anon error:
 details - {"error":"Security Guardrail Triggered: Malicious MCP detected. Invalid tools:
 mcp__plugin_figma_figma__download_assets, … mcp__session__BashOutput, …
 mcp__session__update_user_preferences","operation":"detect"}"}
```

`claude-custom` refused for an unrelated reason ("There's an issue with the
selected model (openrouter/openai/gpt-5.4)"). **`claude-gateway` — the SHIPPING
DEFAULT card, whose translation branch L0, L5, L6 and L7 each recorded as never
exercised — answered normally, and is what all three cases measured.** So the
live-session lifetime claims are now proven on the default card's route; L6's
own subject (the beta-gated `context_management` negotiation, which only the BYO
`anthropic-messages` route can carry) is still unmeasured there, and this run
does not touch it.

The cases therefore choose their card BY MEASUREMENT: they probe candidates in
turn, starting with `NESSY_CDP_CLAUDE_CARD`, and use the first whose model
completes a turn — reporting every refusal verbatim. A pinned card would have
turned an upstream refusal into a wave-wide BLOCKED and produced no evidence at
all.

### Four things the run found on the way

1. **A user Stop leaves an ERROR row in the transcript.** Every arm whose
   interrupt LANDED produced `[ede_diagnostic] result_type=user
   last_content_type=n/a stop_reason=null` with `role: 'error'`, beside the
   "Response interrupted" info row — and the arm whose Stop arrived too late
   produced neither, which is what ties the row to the interrupt rather than to
   the turn ending. The string is in the shipped `claude` binary and in no file
   of ours (`grep -ran`), so the CLI emits a result our `event-adapter` maps
   through the `provider_error` arm. A deliberate Stop should not read as a
   provider failure to the user. Not scored by the case — it does not touch the
   claim — but it is a live defect and it belongs with L2's terminal-reason
   mapping.
2. **"Response interrupted" is not evidence that anything was interrupted.**
   `SessionManager.cancelProcessing` appends it when the user ASKS, before the
   interrupt reaches the child. `LIV-2` records it and asserts on the child's own
   `[Request interrupted by user]` entry instead — diffed before and after the
   arm, so a marker left by anything earlier cannot be read as this run's.
3. **A failed turn respawns the child, and the successor resumes the same
   conversation id.** Seen on `claude-custom-2`: pid 91873 → 92026 across two
   403s, both carrying `--resume=38632224-…`. This is the concrete reason the
   session id cannot be the detector.
4. **An interrupted turn's partial text survives in the child's transcript and
   not in ours.** Seen once, in an ad-hoc probe that stopped at +3.6 s: the SDK
   transcript held the assistant's partially-written list while our session
   transcript held only the info row. Not reproduced in the recorded `LIV-2`
   runs, which stopped at +3.3 s and produced no assistant entry on either side,
   so it is recorded as an observation and not as a finding.
5. **L3's reaper works on the product's route, and the cost it exists for is
   real.** A wave of these cases leaves one live ~300 MB-RSS child per session it
   touched: 16 were alive at once, and `ps` totalled 5.4 GB RSS across them.
   Nothing older than the 30-minute `DEFAULT_IDLE_AGENT_TTL_MS` survived —
   children started between 10:22 and 11:00 were all gone by 11:29 while those
   from 11:08 on were alive — so the idle reaper is visibly retiring them, and
   the `holdsWakePath` rule is not silently exempting every Claude session as
   feared. This is an observation from the side of the wave, not a case: a
   dedicated reaper case needs an idle hour or a knob to shorten it.

### An existing case this plan makes more fragile

`cru-1.ts:422` calls `readClaudeSessionLog(workspaceId)` with **no session id**,
which returns the single newest `.jsonl` across the WHOLE workspace. That was
already documented as "the fallback for a case that does not know which session
it drove"; under the live session it is worse, because sibling sessions now keep
live children that go on writing their transcripts for minutes. The direction of
the error is safe — CRU-1 uses the read to check the SDK's stamped `cwd` and
would BLOCK, not pass — but it should pass `key.sessionId` like `CRU-2`,
`CRU-5` and `COMPACTION-LIFECYCLE` already do. Nothing else in the wave asserts
on Claude session lifetime, and no case was deleted or disabled.

### What could not be driven, by name

- **The two BYO Claude cards.** `claude-custom-2` is refused by the proxy
  guardrail (L10, owner-deferred twice) and `claude-custom`'s model is
  unavailable, so nothing in this wave ran on the `anthropic-messages`
  passthrough route. Everything here is the `claude-gateway` translation branch.
- **A packaged build.** These ran on `electron:dev`, which the harness README
  warns is not the shipped artifact. Nothing measured here reads a log, so the
  dev/packaged difference should not matter — but it was not verified.
- **A model switch that LEAVES the derived shape.** L4's respawn arm — into
  Haiku or out of the Claude family — needs a card offering two models with
  different derived shapes; both `claude-gateway` models share one, so `LIV-3`
  uses the `/clear` respawn as its control instead and BLOCKS, naming the four
  fields, on a card with no same-shaped pair.
- **Coalescing, the queue, background tasks, the reaper and the idle policy**
  (L5, L8, L9, L3). None of them is touched here; a reaper case needs an idle
  hour or a knob to shorten it.
- **Windows.** The detector is `ps`/`lsof` and is macOS/Linux-shaped.

### The neighbouring waves, run on the same rig at the same close-out

Run to check the session-lifecycle rewrite did not break what already worked.
**Two readings here are about the RIG and are recorded as such rather than as
regressions** — the trap this repo enforces is that a live case's detector is the
thing most likely to be wrong.

- **`AGT-1`…`AGT-10`:** `AGT-2`, `AGT-3`, `AGT-9`, `AGT-10` PASS. `AGT-4`, `AGT-5`,
  `AGT-6`, `AGT-7`, `AGT-8` BLOCKED, each naming the env var and fixture the
  harness deliberately does not install — their pre-existing state, unchanged.
  **`AGT-1` is LOAD-SENSITIVE, not broken:** it reported 8/10 inside a ten-case
  wave and then **10/10 on its own**, with the fork registering, answering with
  the parent-only codeword and that codeword appearing in the child's own
  transcript. The wave ran with sixteen Claude children still alive from the LIV
  runs; the accumulation is the likelier cause than the code, and the isolated
  pass is the measurement. Clear stale children before a wave: they are
  attributable by `ps -Ao pid,command | grep -- "--input-format stream-json"`,
  and Claude Desktop's own agent CLI matches that pattern too — leave those.
- **`RTS-*`:** `RTS-5`, `RTS-7`, `RTS-8`, `RTS-11`, `RTS-12` PASS. `RTS-1`,
  `RTS-2`, `RTS-3`, `RTS-6` FAIL **on this rig's precondition and not on the
  product**, and the fixture says so in its own words: the wave wants the
  **PACKAGED** app launched against its own config dir — *"never `electron:dev`
  — the interceptor path and `isPackaged` differ, and this wave is about both"*.
  This rig was `electron:dev` on the operator's normal config dir, which is
  correct for `LIV`/`AGT` and wrong for these four: `RTS-1`'s own error is
  `config-dir-mismatch: no session directory at …/nessy-cdp-rts/config/sessions/…`,
  and `RTS-2`/`RTS-3` fail on file detectors under that same absent tree.
  The five that passed are the ones reading no fixture file. Re-running the four
  needs `bun run scripts/cdp/fixtures/runtime-switch.ts` and its launch recipe;
  they belong to the 2026-08-15 RTS gate below, not to this plan.

## Run of 2026-08-17 — `CRU-1`…`CRU-6`, the clean-runtime plan's live half

Drivers `scripts/cdp/cases/cru-*.ts`, on the operator's real profile against
their own gateway cards, `electron:dev` with the host bundle rebuilt first and
verified to carry the code under test. Every tracker note on that plan that said
"the behavioural half belongs to a live case" is discharged here, and the day
also refuted two rows that had been recorded as done.

**Run it with `ulimit -n 65536`.** The first attempt died on
`EMFILE: too many open files` reading `config.json`, which surfaced as a product
error and is not one: a GUI-launched process inherits launchd's 256-descriptor
default, and a session-heavy run opens far more than that briefly. The app
returns to ~140 descriptors when idle — measured, after an earlier reading of
52 261 was nearly filed as a leak.

| Case | Status | What it settles |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `CRU-1` | **PASS** | Safe Mode on Claude ran a read-only command with NO card, and the operator's own `ask` rule raised one. Attributed rather than assumed: the card offers no "Always Allow", which only the `canUseTool` route produces, so the rule raised it and not our own gate. This is R1+R2+R3's behavioural half. |
| `CRU-2` | **PASS**, after finding R16 | The badge fell 62 660 → 2 782, matching the runtime's own recorded post-compaction figure. It FAILED first, and the failure is the day's biggest finding — see below. |
| `CRU-3` | **PASS** | Pi → Codex lists five costs in rank order; the return leg onto Pi lists none. The control is the point: a cost is what the SOURCE has and the target lacks, so the empty direction is what proves the list is derived rather than written. |
| `CRU-4` | **PASS**, and REWRITTEN 2026-08-17 — see the note under this table | Originally: every mode on a Codex session carries the note for what Codex can enforce, Pi carries none, and the Pi rendering is a strict PREFIX of the Codex one. **That model was correct and is now wrong**, because the shared caveat moved out of the six items into a single footer of the menu. Re-verified in the new shape, 10/10: the caveat appears exactly ONCE as a footer, appears on no item, is absent entirely on Pi (no empty band), a Codex row still EXTENDS its Pi twin rather than rewriting it, and it grows only for the two modes where Codex enforces less than the mode promises. |
| `CRU-5` | **PASS** | A Claude subagent whose definition declares a skill returned the literal that exists only inside that skill's body; the control agent, declaring none, returned `NO-MARKER-AVAILABLE` rather than guessing. |
| `CRU-6` | see below | Isolation. The parent model delegated to the control agent for both arms, so the case refused to score what ran. |

### What the run found that no unit suite could

`SessionManager.sendMessage` processes a `complete` event and then RETURNS out
of the whole function — the comment on the line says "Exit function, skip finally
block" — and its `finally` unwinds the backend generator. **Anything a backend
yields after `complete` is discarded.** Both halves of the previous day's work
sat there:

- the deferred compaction never ran, so the model was told "queued; it will
  happen when this turn ends" and it never happened — a promise not kept, which
  is worse than the error it replaced;
- the context reassert never emitted, so the badge read empty beside a banner
  announcing the compaction.

Both suites were green because a unit test drains the generator and the session
does not. Recorded as R16 on the plan and fixed at the `complete` yield itself.

### Four case defects the run also found, all fixed

None of these was a product fault, and each would have been read as one:

1. `RTS-11` counted a palette row whose DESCRIPTION contained "loop" — so
   `/gsd-plan-phase` scored as a `/loop` entry on every runtime and the case
   reported an offer that had in fact been withdrawn. It matches the command
   token now.
**`CRU-4` WAS BROKEN BY A LATER CHANGE AND ITS ROW STILL SAID PASS — recorded because that is the failure this checklist exists to prevent.** The case hardcoded the three long `chat.permissionModeNote.*` strings and asserted, for all six picker modes, that the Codex-minus-Pi delta equalled one of them. When the picker was reduced to one line per mode those keys were deleted and the caveat became a footer, so every mode failed — while the row above still read PASS. A reviewer found it by grepping the deleted keys, not by running the case. Two lessons kept here rather than in a commit message: a case that hardcodes a user-facing STRING is coupled to a copy edit, which is deliberate here (a loose fragment would pass on a sentence that had lost its meaning) and therefore has to be updated in the same change; and a checklist row is a claim about the code as it was, so a change that invalidates a case must edit the row, not only the case.

2. `CRU-3`/`CRU-4` read authentication from `listLlmConnections`, where the
   status field is ABSENT rather than false, so both blocked on a precondition
   the operator had already met. The tell was `CRU-1` running a complete turn on
   the very card the probe called unauthenticated.
3. `CRU-3` read `messageCount` from the session listing, which derives it from an
   array the listing does not carry, and so reported zero messages for a turn
   whose reply was in its own observations.
4. `CRU-5` refused any agent whose catalog state was not `available`. No agent
   carries that value — all fourteen read `active` — so it blocked on the healthy
   state and called it a refusal.

### One measurement lesson worth more than a case

The first diagnostic added to trace `CRU-2` produced no output at all, and that
absence was nearly read as "the boundary message never arrives". It does arrive.
`this.debug` does not reach the log on that path — zero occurrences against 27
for the module-level `debug` — so the detector was broken, not the mechanism.
**An absence is not evidence until something on the same rig produces a
presence.**

### `CRU-6` — blocked, and the reason is the fixture rather than the runtime

The case needs an installed agent declaring `isolation: worktree` and a control
declaring none. Both were created for the run
(`~/.nessy-agent/agents/cru-isolated.md`, `cru-plain.md`, plus
`cru-house-marker` for `CRU-5`).

**Two attempts, and the second gave the sharper diagnosis.** The first showed
the control agent dispatched for both arms and was read as the model choosing
the wrong `subagent_type`. The second, after the control agent was given the
write tools it had been missing, showed the dispatch itself is fine — the
subagent is recorded as `executing` with an empty result and simply never
returns inside the 300 s turn. The parent's reply is empty both times. So this
is the gateway model failing to complete a delegated turn, not a mis-dispatch
and not a product fault.

The case refuses to score whatever ran rather than attributing another agent's
behaviour to the isolated one, which is correct: a delegation to a different
agent would have measured that agent's isolation.

**What this leaves unproven, stated narrowly.** R12's forwarding is proven at
the seam (`claude-runtime-agents-seam.test.ts`, where removing the one wiring
line reddens the test) and the pinned binary's own consumption site exists — it
creates the worktree and refuses to launch outside git, which a decorative field
could not do. What no run has yet shown is the end-to-end write LOCATION.

**A third attempt ran on `claude-custom-2` — an Anthropic model rather than the
gateway's — and produced a finding of its own, unrelated to isolation.** That
card reaches `anthropic/claude-fable-5` through `llm-proxy.t-tech.team`, and
every turn is refused before it reaches the model:

> `API Error: 403 … "Security Guardrail Triggered: Malicious MCP detected.
> Invalid tools: mcp__plugin_figma_figma__…, mcp__session__BashOutput,
> mcp__session__SubmitPlan, mcp__session__TodoWrite, mcp__session__browser_tool,
> mcp__session__call_llm, …"`

The list runs to roughly fifty entries and it is **our own session-tool surface**,
plus the figma plugin's. On that proxy the guardrail rejects Nessy's tools
wholesale, at authentication, and the turn dies with no model involvement at all.
Not intermittent, not model-dependent: the same request shape fails every time.

Two consequences worth separating:

- **For this case:** neither available Claude card can run it. The gateway card's
  model does not finish a delegated turn; the proxy card's requests never reach a
  model. Owner instruction 2026-08-17 is to skip rather than work around it.
- **For the product, and this is the larger one:** a user on an Anthropic model
  behind that proxy cannot use Nessy's session tools at all. Whether the answer
  is to trim what we advertise, to let a connection opt out of the session MCP
  server, or to take it up with the proxy's owners is a product question rather
  than a testing one. Recorded here because the live run is where it surfaced.

Nothing about the case needs changing; it wants a runtime where a delegated turn
both starts and finishes.

## Run of 2026-08-15 — `RTS-1`…`RTS-12`, session runtime switching

Drivers `scripts/cdp/cases/rts-*.ts`, fixture
`scripts/cdp/fixtures/runtime-switch.ts`, on a **packaged `.app`** built from the
tree under test. Bundle verified to carry the code before a prompt was sent:
`switchSessionRuntime` 8, `runtimeHandoffFrom` 8, `sdkSessionAnchors` 14,
`clearRuntimeScopedSessionState` 3, `exitWorktreeBeforeRuntimeSwitch` 2,
`appendRuntimeSwitchNotice` 2, `describeCapabilityDelta` 2, and
`dist/interceptor.cjs` present at 70 215 bytes — a packaged run with a missing
interceptor starts Pi **unpatched and silent**.

**The dmg step failed and that is expected, not a workaround.**
`⨯ cannot expand pattern "${env.NESSY_DESKTOP_UPDATE_URL}"` — the publish block
wants an env var a local build has no business supplying. electron-builder had
already written `release/mac-arm64/Nessy Desktop.app`, and the `.app` is what
carries `isPackaged`. Same failure and same conclusion as the 2026-08-11 sitting
recorded further down this file.

### The run cannot use the operator's real config dir, and that is measured

`loadShellEnv()` skips only when `VITE_DEV_SERVER_URL` is set — i.e. only in dev.
On the packaged `.app` it spawns `$SHELL -l -i -c env` and copies the login
shell's whole environment over `process.env`, PATH included, before
`getStableMachineId()` runs. So `ioreg` always resolves, the v2-stable key is
always derived, and `~/.nessy-agent/credentials.enc` — which decrypts under the
`username:homedir` FALLBACK id — is unreadable to it. Nothing is damaged; all 11
entries are intact. Lane K of the base-context-diet plan owns the fix.

The wave therefore runs against its own `NESSY_CONFIG_DIR`, seeded with the
owner's own three gateway cards copied read-only. **Those three ARE the three
runtimes this plan switches between**, and they need no credential store — they
authenticate from `~/.nessy/dp_auth_creds.json` (JWT valid to 2028-07-04), which
lives outside the config dir and is never written by the harness. So the core
claim is provable on a packaged build against the real gateway even though most
BYO connections on this machine are not readable. The cards carry no secret in
config at all: `slug`, `name`, `providerType`, `authType`, `baseUrl`, `models`,
`defaultModel` and nothing else.

| Case | Verdict | The one-line fact |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `RTS-1` Codex model change | **PASS 7/7** | Label planted, three turns, model `qwen36-35b` → `qwen35-397b`, label returned exactly (`7483F-B7AE4-19C1A-74116`). Pre-T1 the thread was disposed and recreated blank. Connection asserted unchanged either side — this is the model-only tier. |
| `RTS-2` Pi → Claude handoff | **PASS 11/11** | Pi edited two real files (`M counter.ts`, `M greeting.ts`, measured with `git status --porcelain`, not from the model's summary); after the switch Claude named both AND returned the label planted on Pi (`60446-A18AD-9AD41-8B2D5`). The label is the load-bearing half: file names alone are confoundable, because an agent bound to the repo can answer "what did we change" from `git status` without remembering anything. |
| `RTS-3` return leg | **PASS 12/12** after T18 | **Found a design defect; see below.** Now: the return resumes Pi's own sidecar, both labels survive, the handoff carries the Claude span and no longer repeats the pre-switch one. |
| `RTS-4` Claude Pro/Max → gateway | **BLOCKED**, and reversibly so | The Claude Pro/Max card is **switched off and hidden visually, code retained** (owner decision 2026-08-15) — so no subscription connection exists to switch FROM. **Deferred, not inapplicable:** the row becomes runnable the moment the card is re-enabled. It was briefly deleted here on a stronger reading of that decision ("there will be no subscription"), which the owner did not make; the correction is recorded because a deleted case and a temporarily unavailable one are different states. Creating a connection is a subscription sign-in — a credential action the harness does not perform — and a packaged run would additionally need lane K. Set `NESSY_CDP_CLAUDE_SUB_SLUG` once signed in. |
| `RTS-5` permission enforcement | **PASS 6/6** | `bypassPermissions` set on Codex, switched to Pi, recorded mode still `bypassPermissions`, gated command ran unasked — **record and behaviour agree**. In-run control first: a fresh Pi session in `default` must prompt for the same command, else the case reports BLOCKED. |
| `RTS-6` `/loop` stops off Pi | **PASS 3/3** | The loop fired on Pi (1 → 6 messages), then **zero** turns across 150 s — two full intervals — after the switch. C2 holds. |
| `RTS-7` image across a switch | **PASS 6/6** | `codex-gateway` reports `attachments.images: "yes"`, Pi and Claude `"no"`, so the row runs Codex → Claude; the switch names the file: `droppedAttachments: ["rts7-screenshot.png"]`. |
| `RTS-8` switch mid-turn | **FAIL 4/5** | The refusal itself works and the positive control passes (the same switch is accepted once the turn settles). **The refusal reaches the renderer with no `code`** — see T19 below. |
| `RTS-9` plugin monitor | **BLOCKED** | Needs a plugin bundling a `when: 'always'` monitor; none installed. The harness does not install plugins for itself. Set `NESSY_CDP_MONITOR_PLUGIN` once one is. |
| `RTS-10` MCP always-allow | **BLOCKED** | Needs a callable credential-free MCP tool. The builtin `time` server was enabled by the fixture and reaches `state: active`, but the pool still answers `refused/disconnected`, so no tool can be called. Set `NESSY_CDP_MCP_TOOL` once one is reachable. |
| `RTS-11` `/loop` offered off Pi | **PASS 7/7** after the fix | Was FAIL: `/loop` offered identically on all three runtimes, while its four scheduling tools are `visibility: 'pi-only'` (`tool-defs.ts:1161-1169`, under a comment calling pi-only "a SECURITY constraint, not a preference"). **The first diagnosis was wrong and is corrected below.** |
| `RTS-12` context meter | **PASS 8/8** | Pi's 262 144 window is cleared on the switch and `tokenUsage.contextWindow` with it; after one Codex turn the window is **249 036** and the occupancy is that turn's (17 536, not Pi's 37 299). No threshold warning where `thresholdKind` is `none`. |

### T18 — the defect RTS-3 found, and the fix it took two attempts to get right

Two beliefs are written into `SessionManager.ts` and both are reasonable alone:
`clearRuntimeScopedSessionState` folds the outgoing `sdkSessionId` into that
provider's own anchor slot **so coming back still reaches that conversation**
(`:15308`); the cross-runtime branch arms the text handoff on the premise that
**"the target runtime has no anchor of its own, so the only thing that can cross
is text"** (`:15109`). On a return leg the second is false, and both fire.

Measured before the fix, on packaged 1.26.1 and reproduced on two independent
sessions: Pi appended to the **same** `.pi-sessions` sidecar it wrote before
leaving (count 1 → 1), and both of its pre-switch assistant replies appeared
verbatim inside `<runtime_handoff_context>` on the first turn after the return.
Nothing was lost — this costs context, not correctness — but it lands in the
same window a just-closed plan spent nine tasks accounting for, and it grows
with the conversation.

**The first fix was wrong and the live case caught it.** Skipping the handoff
when the target has an anchor loses the span the OTHER runtime produced while
this one was away — RTS-3's own green half. The shipped fix trims instead:
`getRuntimeHandoffMessages` carries only the messages produced since the target
last spoke.

**And the trim's first boundary was also wrong, which no unit test could see.**
Keying on provenance alone put the boundary at the END of the transcript on
every return leg, because the user message being sent is appended before
`chat()` composes the parts and is stamped with the provider about to answer it.
`slice` then yielded nothing and the handoff silently vanished — measured: a
returning Pi session answered *"there is no mention of a secret fruit in our
conversation"* about a fact planted on Claude two turns earlier. The boundary is
now the last **assistant** row with that provenance; a runtime produces assistant
rows, not user ones. **The unit rows passed against both broken versions** — they
assert the classification, and the trim lives in a closure inside
`getOrCreateAgent`. The live case is what covered it.

### RTS-11 — the first diagnosis was wrong, and the wrong one was the tidier story

The row was first written up as "the palette has no runtime filter", on the
strength of `slash.commandIds: "all"` in the capability snapshot. That is true
and irrelevant: `commandIds` describes the NATIVE command set, which genuinely
is the same everywhere. The skill half is decided elsewhere, and **the filter
has existed since T17** — `withoutLoopSkillWhereCronToolsAreAbsent`, applied to
the slash-command list and to the model-facing runtime view.

The real defect was one layer out: the server withheld the skill correctly and
**the renderer never asked again**. `refreshCustomSlashCommands` re-ran on
workspace, session and working-directory changes, and not on a connection
change, so after a mid-session switch the pre-switch list stayed on screen and
`/loop` remained offered on a runtime that cannot honour it. Fixed by adding the
session's connection to that effect's dependencies; re-measured 7/7, offered on
Pi and withdrawn on Claude and Codex.

Two lessons, both paid for here:

- **A capability that "looks unfiltered" is not evidence that filtering is
  absent** — check whether something ELSE applies it before concluding the
  mechanism does not exist.
- **A stale client is indistinguishable from a wrong server** from the outside.
  Measuring the palette is right for the user-facing claim, and that same
  reading is compatible with two very different causes.

The corrected row also retired an assertion: `slash.commandIds !== 'all'` stayed
RED after the real fix landed, because it asserted an implementation shape
rather than what the user sees. It is an observation now.

### Rig facts this run added

- **A filesystem detector must assert its own root exists, or it is a constant
  that always says "absent".** `memoryLeaks` defaults `configDir` to
  `~/.nessy-agent`; a fixture run that does not pass the real one scans three
  files that do not exist, returns `[]`, and that reads as "the label did not
  leak" — a false all-clear licensing a recall the case should have refused to
  score. `requireSessionDir` now throws instead.
- **A detector that cannot fail cannot pass either.** RTS-5's first version
  measured "the shell command ran without a prompt" and was one commit from
  filing *privileged mode survives a runtime switch* — a security finding. The
  control refuted it: a fresh Pi session in `explore` ran the same command
  unasked, because `echo` is classified read-only and auto-approved in **every**
  mode. Measured ladder, in `default`: `echo …` → **RAN UNASKED**;
  `touch <file> && echo …` → **ASKED**; `printf … > <file>` → **ASKED**;
  `curl … example.com` → **ASKED**. The next person reaching for a shell command
  as a permission probe will reach for `echo` too.
- **The plan's premise for that row was wrong in the safer direction.** It
  asserted Pi may not run in `bypassPermissions` and "MUST prompt". Pi supports
  the mode, and `renormalizeForRuntime` only rewrites what the TARGET disallows.
  The replacement invariant is weaker to state and holds whatever the policy
  says: **behaviour must agree with whatever mode the record shows after the
  switch; drift in either direction is the failure.**
- **An error's SHAPE must be asserted at the boundary the user's code reads it
  from, not where it is thrown.** See T19.
- **A duplication detector must key on something unique to the span it
  attributes.** RTS-3's first duplication check compared reply text by substring
  and reported a duplicate that was not one: both spans answer their plant
  prompt with `OK`. It keys on the per-run labels now.
- **A pipeline can launder an exit code.** `…build-dmg.sh; echo "EXIT=$?" | tee -a log`
  reports the *command's* status, which is `tee`'s 0, while the build exited 1.
  Same shape as the `typecheck:all`-after-a-pipe trap already recorded here.
- **The trust modal owns the screen.** Binding a session to a directory with no
  trust decision raises a modal, and every case that types afterwards fails with
  `composer-not-focusable` — a message that says nothing about a dialog.
- **`/loop` is self-executing, not prefilling**, so `selectSlashCommand` cannot
  carry its argument; RTS-6 types it, and the firing control is what proves it
  was taken as a command rather than prose.

### T19 — the refusal codes never reach the renderer

`ChatPage.tsx:355` picks the toast by `error.code`: `SESSION_BUSY` → "a turn is
running", `WORKTREE_DIRTY` → "commit or stash first",
`WORKTREE_EXIT_UNAVAILABLE` → "move out of the worktree", anything else → the
generic "switch failed". Measured at the renderer through
`window.electronAPI.sessionCommand` — the exact call that handler makes — three
ways, with a control: `getOwnPropertyNames` → `["stack","message"]`,
`hasOwnProperty('code')` → **false**, constructor `Error`.

**The transport is innocent.** `server.ts:688` maps a `CodedError` through
`isErrorCode`, `SESSION_BUSY` and `WORKTREE_DIRTY` are both members of the wire
`ErrorCode` union, and `client.ts:594-598` re-attaches `err.code`. The loss is at
**`contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld`** (`preload/bootstrap.ts:492`).

**Three channels are now measured shut, and the third took a failed fix to
find.** A stack-marker fix was written, shipped into a packaged build, and was
**inert**: the stack does not cross either.

| channel | survives? | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| own property `code` | **no** | `getOwnPropertyNames` → `["stack","message"]`, `hasOwnProperty('code')` false |
| `name` | **no** | `CodedError` sets `name = 'CodedError'`; the renderer reads `Error` |
| `stack` | **no** | REGENERATED, not copied — the renderer receives `Error: <message>` as a **single line**, `stackLines: 1`, no frames |
| `message` | yes | and it is translated and user-visible, which is why it is not a channel |

So a general fix at the boundary that keeps the value an `Error` is not
available: the only surviving field is the one the user reads. The remaining
shapes each cost something real — rejecting with a plain object breaks the 64
`instanceof Error` sites in the renderer; encoding the code into `message` puts
a machine token in front of a user. **The fix therefore needs a design decision,
not more implementation**, and the inert attempt was reverted rather than left
in the tree looking like coverage.

So all three specific messages are unreachable on the packaged build and every
refusal shows the generic one. The worktree-dirty case is the one that stings:
it is the only thing telling a user their uncommitted work is why the switch
failed. Host-side tests call `switchSessionRuntime` directly, where the error is
intact — which is why this survived to a live run.

**Not fixed here.** The fix is a cross-cutting transport change touching every
`catch` in the renderer, and the obvious shapes each cost something: rejecting
with a plain object breaks `instanceof Error` for existing handlers; encoding the
code into the message is text-sniffing, and the text is i18n'd and will be
reworded. Scoped as its own task with this diagnosis attached.

Only `SESSION_BUSY` was reproduced. The other two ride the same channel so the
same loss follows, but they were not measured.

## Run of 2026-08-15 — F5 `COMPACT-LIFE`, the compaction lifecycle per runtime

Driver `scripts/cdp/cases/compaction-lifecycle.ts`, one arm per run
(`NESSY_F5_RUNTIME=pi|claude|codex`), on the owner's real profile against a
**run-owned** gateway connection the case creates and deletes. The owner's own
connections are never read-modified: the narrow window is written into the new
connection's `models[]` entry, which is the product path C2/F2/F3 all consume,
and the gateway JWT is fetched and spent inside ONE page-side expression so no
credential value ever reaches the driver process. The case refuses to start if
its slug already exists.

**The claude and codex arms were re-run the same day against F6 (`16b4445c6`)
and F7 (`754efa7fd`)**, the two product fixes the first sitting produced, **and
claude a third time against F9 (`9fabd03d6`)**, which is the sitting that finally
turned its auto half green. Every block those sittings found is gone; the table
below carries the final verdicts, and the arms' earlier text is kept underneath
because the reasoning in it is still the reason the case is shaped the way it is.
Three claude runs were needed before the third sitting and the reason is a
detector fault worth reading — see *A THRESHOLD IS A BAND*. Every run below is on
a rebuilt `main.cjs` verified to carry the fix under test before a single prompt
was sent (`recordAssistantUsage`, `AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW_FRACTION`, and for the
third sitting `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` — 0 occurrences in the bundle
that was on disk, 1 in the rebuilt one), and on an app launched with `/usr/sbin`
out of `PATH` per the `getStableMachineId` trap recorded at the end of this
section.

| Arm | Verdict | The one-line fact |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `COMPACT-LIFE` pi | **PASS 10/10** | The whole lifecycle, end to end. Window 240 000 read back as `contextWindowSource: 'runtime'`, threshold 206 808; manual `/compact` at 132 377 → **108 433**; four more turns to 217 363, auto-compaction fired and the context came down to **120 972**. Both compactions are in the session's own sidecar with their own `tokensBefore` (132 377 and **217 363**, the latter above the 206 808 threshold), and Pi labels them apart: `fromHook: false` manual, `true` automatic. |
| `COMPACT-LIFE` claude | **PASS 13/13** (third sitting, against F9 — see below) | The whole lifecycle, after two sittings that could not get the second half. Manual `/compact` **123 794 → 59 214** with the SDK's own `compact_boundary` (`trigger: "manual"`, `preTokens: 123 819`), then five filler turns to 204 509 and a calibrated sixth — and the SDK compacted **on its own**: a second boundary, `{trigger: "auto", preTokens: 217844, postTokens: 14748}`, 10 844 tokens past the 207 000 point F4 displays, occupancy **204 509 → 66 229**, error-row diff empty. F9's `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` is what changed; the same fill reached 227 337 with no compaction before it. |
| `COMPACT-LIFE` codex | **PASS 9/9** (re-run against F7 — see below) | The whole lifecycle. `model_auto_compact_token_limit = 204000` read out of the live session's own `CODEX_HOME/config.toml` beside `model_context_window = 240000`; manual `/compact` **119 415 → 63 635**; five more turns to **208 660** and auto-compaction fired on its own, **208 660 → 71 824** (−136 836), 4 660 tokens past the written limit, no error rows. The first sitting's "codex never auto-compacts" is closed. |

### The owner-pinned shape needed a correction, and the product is what said so

"Fill to half the window, then `/compact`" is not runnable as stated on this
profile. Compaction keeps the last **`keepRecentTokens` = 20 000 tokens of
CONVERSATION** and summarises what is older (`prepareCompaction` in
`@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent/dist/core/compaction/compaction.js`;
`agent-session.js` throws **"Nothing to compact (session too small)"** when
nothing is left on the older side of the cut). The resting base — 70 571
measured this run — is system prompt and tool schemas, which compaction never
touches, so half a window is a compactable point only when
`window / 2 − base > keepRecentTokens`.

Measured, not reasoned: on a 180 000 window half is 90 000, the session sat at
**91 325** with ~20 500 tokens of conversation, and `/compact` answered
`Nothing to compact (session too small)` as a transcript ERROR row. The case now
derives its floor from that rule — `MIN_WINDOW = 2 × (base + 2 × keepRecentTokens)`
= 221 646 — and defaults to 240 000, where half leaves ~61 600 tokens of real
conversation above the base and three paste turns reach it.

### What the three arms say about each runtime, in one place

| | window from the catalog entry | occupancy counter | manual `/compact` | auto-compaction |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| pi | ✅ 240 000, `source: 'runtime'` | ✅ `contextUsage.inputTokens` | ✅ −23 944 | ✅ at 217 363 ≥ 206 808, −96 391 |
| claude | ✅ 240 000 (F2 live-proven) | ✅ `tokenUsage.inputTokens`, 58 396 (F6) | ✅ −64 521, with the SDK's own `compact_boundary` | ✅ at `preTokens` 217 844 ≥ 207 000, −138 280, `trigger: "auto"` (F9) |
| codex | ✅ 240 000 written to config.toml; reported as 228 000 (5% reserve) | ✅ `tokenUsage.inputTokens` | ✅ −55 780 | ✅ at 208 660 ≥ 204 000, −136 836 (F7) |

Every cell in that table was a product finding at some point in this cycle, and
all three were fixed and re-measured rather than recorded and left: F6 gave claude
an occupancy counter, F7 gave codex a limit to fire on, and F9 gave claude's
auto-compaction a window source its own gate would act on. The claude column took
three sittings; the reasoning of each is kept below in the order it was learned.

**Claude reported no context occupancy at all — FIXED by F6, re-measured here.**
The first sitting read `tokenUsage.inputTokens: 0` after a completed turn while
the SDK's own `modelUsage` entry for that same turn carried 58 392, on this run's
session and on the owner's own (0 against 58 377 / 86 540 / 86 554). The plan's
candidate cause (`isSidechain = parent_tool_use_id !== null` reading an absent
field as a sidechain) was REFUTED by F6: the binary sends `parent_tool_use_id:
null` explicitly. The real cause was the FRAME — the assistant message's
`message.usage` is the `message_start` snapshot, all zeros on a wire that reports
usage only in its trailing chunk, and `adaptResult` preferred that zero. The
adapter now reads `message_delta` too. **Live on the re-run: 58 396 after the
first turn**, and it tracked every filler turn from there (80 182 → 101 982 →
123 782 → …), which is what made the rest of this arm measurable at all.

**Codex never auto-compacted — FIXED by F7, re-measured here.** The first sitting
took a session to 208 731 of its 228 000 window and a settling turn moved it up,
not down, because F3 deliberately wrote no `model_auto_compact_token_limit` and
codex's own default did not fire. F7 writes it from the same synced window as
`model_context_window`, at 0.85 of it. **Live on the re-run: the key is on disk**
in the running session's own `CODEX_HOME/config.toml` —

```toml
model_context_window = 240000
model_auto_compact_token_limit = 204000
```

— and the net fired: the fill reached **208 660** and the next turn came back at
**71 824**, a 136 836-token drop with no error row anywhere near it.

**`model_auto_compact_token_limit_scope` is settled: the default charges the FULL
context.** F7 left the scope at codex's own default and recorded that its value is
undocumented — the schema offers `total` ("count the full active context") and
`body_after_prefix` ("count sampled output and later growth after the carried
window prefix"), and the bundled binary carries both strings with no default this
rig can read out of it. The drop point decides it. This session's resting base was
32 622 tokens, so under `body_after_prefix` the charge at the trigger would have
been about 176 000 — well under the 204 000 limit — and nothing would have fired.
It fired at 208 660 of total context, 4 660 past the limit. **The default behaves
as `total`, which is the assumption F7's 0.85 was sized under**, and F7's
"under-set on purpose" hedge turns out not to have been needed.

**FINDING, CAUSE, FIX, AND PROOF — the Claude runtime had manual compaction and
no automatic one; F9 fixed it and the third sitting measured the fix.** The ❌ in
the table above is now a ✅, and the paragraphs below are kept in the order they
were learned: what the second sitting measured, what the binary said, what the
fix was, and what the re-run read back. With the
SDK believing a 240 000 window (its own `modelUsage.contextWindow`, F2) and F4's
`formula-claude` putting its compaction point at 207 000, the session was carried
to **225 523 and then 227 337 — 94.7% of that window, 20 337 tokens past the
point** — and nothing happened: no drop, no second `compact_boundary` (the count
stayed at the 1 the manual command wrote), no error rows. The positive control is
in the same session on the same runtime: `/compact` typed by hand DID compact,
123 782 → 59 261, and the SDK wrote its own record for it
(`compactMetadata: {trigger: "manual", preTokens: 123816, postTokens: 2135}`).
So the instrument works and the affordance a user reaches for works; the
automatic safety net is what is absent. The product does not disable it either —
`DISABLE_AUTO_COMPACT` is read only on the Pi path
(`config/auto-compaction-settings.ts`) and is never set for the Claude
subprocess.

**THE CAUSE, read out of the binary we ship (F9).** The SDK's only proactive
auto-compaction gate is one function, and its fourth line is the whole finding
(`claude-agent-sdk-darwin-arm64/claude`, fn `pY_`):

```js
if (dor(n)) return !1;
if (ylt(n)) return !1;
if (!QI()) return !1;
if (Vbe() && !cGe(t, r)) return !1;          // ← here
let s = WA(e, vw(t)) - o, a = wFe(s, t, r);
return a.level === "compact" || a.level === "blocked"
```

`cGe(model, window)` is `NK(model, window).source !== "auto"`, and `NK` walks
env → settings → clientdata → experiment → a three-id table of known Claude
models → `{window: <the model's max>, source: "auto"}`. A gateway id falls to
that last arm, `cGe` is false, `Vbe()` is true (it only goes false under
`CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE`), and the caller `pRs` therefore answers
`{kind: "not_needed"}` on every single turn. **`source === "auto"` is not
inferred:** the SDK reported 240 000 in this very run, which is `NKc`'s env
value — had the known-id arm matched, `JE` would have returned its 200 000
constant instead. Nothing else rescues it: the post-turn reactive ARMING path
sits behind a remote gate that defaults off (`vtn` → `Je("tengu_sepia_moth",
!1)`), and the surviving net (`OSo` from the PTL branch) fires only on a
recognised `prompt_too_long` API error — which this gateway's 400 does not
present as, the very defect the next finding describes. The three mechanisms
fail in a row, which is why the session simply kept growing.

The current docs describe the behaviour we did NOT get. `model-config.md`
§ *Default auto-compact thresholds*: "Sessions on a model ID Claude Code doesn't
recognize, such as an LLM gateway alias, compact at the context window Claude
Code assumes for the ID." The bundled binary is **v2.1.218** (the version string
is in the bundle), and `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_UNKNOWN_MODEL_WINDOW_ENFORCEMENT` —
the opt-OUT the same docs add for that case, marked "Requires Claude Code
v2.1.223 or later" — appears **zero times** in it. The page is ahead of the
binary; the binary is what ships.

**THE FIX.** `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`, set from the same stored window
and behind the same gate as F2's `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONTEXT_TOKENS`
(`buildClaudeAutoCompactWindowEnv`). It is `NK`'s FIRST branch, so the source
becomes `"env"`, `cGe` becomes true, and `pY_` goes on to the threshold check —
whose compact level lands at `window − min(maxOutput, 20 000) − 13 000`, the
identical subtraction `context-meter-state.ts` already draws the composer's
warning against. The number the user sees becomes the number the SDK acts on:
**207 000 on this 240 000 window**, the point this run sailed 20 337 past.
Documented, `model-config.md` § *Set the auto-compact window*: "In scripts and
cloud environments: set `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`. While it's set, it
takes precedence over the command, the flag, and the setting".

**WHAT THE RE-RUN READ BACK — the SDK compacted on its own, 10 844 tokens past
the number the composer draws.** `NESSY_F5_RUNTIME=claude` unchanged, third
sitting, **PASS 13/13**. The assertion the first two sittings could not satisfy is
in the SDK's own transcript:

```json
"compactMetadata": {"trigger": "auto", "preTokens": 217844, "postTokens": 14748,
                    "durationMs": 5845, "cumulativeDroppedTokens": 324887}
```

Two boundaries in one session and the runtime labels them apart — `"manual"` at
`preTokens` 123 819 for the `/compact` the case typed, `"auto"` at 217 844 for the
one nobody asked for — so the positive control and the subject sit side by side in
one file. Occupancy **204 509 → 66 229** (−138 280) and the **error-row diff across
the whole auto half is empty**, which is what separates this from the silent
conversation reset below: that one drops further and writes an error row first.
The fill was identical to the second sitting's, which reached 227 337 with one
boundary and no drop.

**The free stderr detector does not work — do not plan a run around it.** The gate
logs `autocompact: tokens=… level=… effectiveWindow=…` *after* the `cGe` line, so
its absence-then-presence should have been the gate flipping regardless of
outcome. It never appeared: zero hits on the app's stderr and zero in
`~/Library/Logs/Nessy Desktop/main.log`, through a run in which the gate
demonstrably ran and compacted twice. The binary emits that line through a
minified logger whose own gating could not be read out of the bundle, and the
subprocess wrote nothing to stderr at all this run (`[SDK stderr]`: 0 lines), so
the silence says nothing in either direction. **A detector that cannot produce a
positive is not a detector** — the transcript the feature writes for itself is the
one that settled this, as it was the one that settled every earlier round.

**AND THE RUNTIME'S OWN `preTokens` IS WHAT MADE THE THRESHOLD ASSERTION
POSSIBLE.** The sampled peak was 204 509 — *below* the 207 000 point — while the
record's own figure for the very same event was 217 844. The gap is the known lag
(the sample is taken after a turn completes; the compaction happens on the next
request, whose input is larger), and a case asserting on its sample would have
reported a correct trigger as an early one. `tokensBefore()` reads
`compactMetadata.preTokens` for exactly this reason.

**One driver change came out of this sitting: a second record is not yet an
automatic one.** The manual `/compact` this case sends writes a `compact_boundary`
of its own, so "the count went up" is satisfied by a user typing the command
twice — the LABEL is what separates the safety net from the affordance. The arm
now asserts it (`compactMetadata.trigger !== "manual"` on claude, `fromHook ===
true` on pi), and an ABSENT label fails on purpose: an unlabelled record cannot
tell the two apart, and defaulting it to "automatic" is how a rig reports the
event it was hoping for.

**NEW FINDING — what a claude session does when it runs out of room, and how it
reads to the user.** The first re-run attempt crossed the band in one step and
caught the consequence. The request went out at **233 764 input tokens**; the
gateway refused it, and its message is exact —

> `API Error: 400 upstream error 400: Requested token count exceeds the model's
> maximum context length of 262144 tokens. You requested a total of 265764
> tokens: 233764 tokens from the input messages and 32000 tokens for the
> completion.`

— so the ceiling that actually binds is the model's real 262 144 **less the
32 000-token completion reserve**, i.e. 230 144 of input, which is BELOW the
240 000 window the SDK was told to believe. Two things then happened, and both
are worth their own row. The transcript row the user sees reads **"Unknown Error:
An unexpected error occurred."** — the quoted sentence above never reaches them.
And 0.6 seconds later the runtime answered the same prompt from a **brand-new
conversation**: a fresh `<uuid>.jsonl` in the same project directory whose first
entry carries `parentUuid: null`, with the whole history gone and no notice. The
occupancy fell 204 685 → 87 118 — a larger fall than any compaction in this case
— **which is exactly what a working auto-compaction looks like from the outside.**

**NEW FINDING — the usage overlay's Context row still reads zero on claude and
codex — FIXED by F8.** F6 fills `tokenUsage.inputTokens`, and the composer's donut reads it
(`ChatDisplay.tsx` feeds `contextStatus.inputTokens` from
`session.tokenUsage?.inputTokens` behind `mayFallBackToTokenUsage`). The overlay
row does not: `formatContextUsage` reads
`tokenUsage?.contextTokens ?? tokenUsage?.inputTokens`, and `contextTokens` is
INITIALISED to 0 by `ensureTokenUsage` and only ever written by
`applyContextUsageSnapshot`, which is the Pi-only path — so `??` never falls
through. Positive control, the real formatter against the tokenUsage this run
measured live: `{inputTokens: 58396, contextTokens: 0, contextWindow: 240000}` →
**"0 / 240,000 (0%)"**, while the same function on a Pi-shaped snapshot returns
"132,377 / 240,000 (55%)". Two surfaces reading one counter two ways.
F8 makes a zero `contextTokens` mean "no observation" at both consumers of that
pair — the overlay row and `getContextTokenTotal`, which also feeds the
`/context` overlay's Current row, its Remaining/Used% and the breakdown's
percentage denominator. The two strings above are the unit test's control
values, and the old expression restored still prints them.

**Codex reports 95% of the window it is given.** `model_context_window = 240000`
in the live session's own `CODEX_HOME/config.toml` (read directly — F3(b)
live-proven) comes back as `modelContextWindow: 228000`. Two independent points
confirm the ratio: the pre-F3 sessions on this profile carry codex's own 272 000
default and report 258 400. Codex holds a 5% reserve back before it tells anyone
the number; the case asserts the reserved figure and says why.

### Detector faults the re-run caught, each of which had already produced a verdict

- **A THRESHOLD IS A BAND, AND A FIXED FILLER STEP JUMPS IT WHOLE.** This is the
  one that manufactured a wrong answer. One 60 000-character filler is 29 075
  tokens on the claude arm; the band there runs from 207 000 (the SDK's own
  compaction point on a 240 000 window) to 230 144 (the largest input the gateway
  accepts once the 32 000-token completion reserve is counted against the model's
  real 262 144) — **23 144 wide, narrower than one step.** The fill went 175 610 →
  204 685, one below the band, and the next identical step asked for 233 764, one
  above it. Nothing was ever offered to the runtime AT a compactable point. The
  case now sizes its last approach turn from the tokens-per-character rate it
  measures for itself during the run (`APPROACH_OVERSHOOT_TOKENS`), and the same
  session then landed on 213 340 and, with a bigger aim, 225 523 — inside the
  band both times, with the runtime still declining to compact. Codex has the
  identical shape (a 204 000 limit under a 228 000 wall is a 24 000 band), and Pi
  simply crosses its threshold by less.
- **The codex arm was aiming BELOW the trigger, by construction.** With no
  threshold of its own it filled to 88% of the window codex REPORTS (0.88 ×
  228 000 = 200 640), while F7's limit is 0.85 of the window that was CONFIGURED
  (0.85 × 240 000 = 204 000). Codex reports 95% of what it is given, so those two
  fractions are 88% against 89.5% of the same number: the old target stopped
  3 360 tokens short of the trigger every time, and the arm would have reported
  "codex never auto-compacts" about a fill that never reached the limit. The
  target is now the limit itself.
- **A DROP IS NOT A COMPACTION.** The case's headline check was satisfied by an
  occupancy that fell — and the fall above came from a refused request and a
  silently restarted conversation, with nothing summarised. It now requires the
  drop AND an empty error-row diff across the auto half, and prints the error
  when there is one. The check that caught the imposter first was the runtime's
  own record ("1 → 0"), which is the whole argument for reading a file the
  feature writes itself.
- **One Nessy session is MANY SDK transcripts.** `readClaudeSessionLog` read the
  NEWEST `.jsonl` in the session's project directory, and the SDK opens a new one
  whenever it starts a fresh conversation — so the boundary count went 1 → 0
  across a step that added none, because the boundary was in the file the reader
  had stopped looking at. Narrowed to one session it now reads every `.jsonl` in
  that directory and reports how many there were; a count above one is itself
  worth seeing.
- **The threshold assertion was describing the wrong event.** `autoRecord.newest`
  is the newest record of ANY kind, so with no automatic compaction it is the
  MANUAL one — and the case printed "the trigger fired early — pre-compaction
  123 843 against a threshold of 207 000" about a trigger that never fired. It is
  now skipped, with its reason stated, unless an automatic record actually exists.
- **The Claude SDK writes its own pre-compaction figure and its own manual/auto
  label, under different names from Pi's.** `compactMetadata.preTokens` and
  `compactMetadata.trigger` against Pi's `tokensBefore` and `fromHook` — read off
  the shipped binary's construction site and confirmed live (`preTokens: 123816`
  against a sampled 123 782). The case reads both now, so the claude arm asserts
  on the runtime's own number rather than on a sample.

### Detector faults caught before any became a finding (first sitting)

- **The occupancy counter does not fall when the compaction does — on EITHER
  half.** A manual `/compact` that demonstrably ran, with a `compaction` entry
  carrying `tokensBefore: 132186` in the session's own sidecar seconds earlier,
  left `contextUsage.inputTokens` reading 132 186 unchanged: that number is the
  PROVIDER's last reported figure (`source: 'sdk_usage'`) and no provider had
  answered since. The automatic half has the identical lag and cost a RED run
  before it was fixed there too — the sidecar showed the automatic compaction at
  `tokensBefore: 217353`, above its 206 808 threshold, while the case reported
  "no drop seen in 4 turns" from the stale sample. Both halves now read the drop
  where a user reads it: one small turn later. The settling turn must be SMALL —
  another 29 000-token filler at that point would overrun codex's window instead
  of reading it — and it is a positive control in its own right, since on Pi it
  turns 217 363 into 120 972 while on codex it moves nothing.
- **`ContextUsageSnapshot.lastCompaction` was null** in the DTO seconds after a
  compaction the same runtime had just written. AGENTS.md already records the
  field as unfilled; this run re-confirmed it on the MANUAL path too.
- **`tokenUsage.contextUsage` exists on Pi and nowhere else.** The snapshot is
  built in `packages/pi-agent-server/src/context-usage.ts`, and
