  `SessionManager.applyContextUsageSnapshot` is the only writer of
  `tokenUsage.contextTokens` — so on claude-runtime and codex-runtime there is
  no snapshot and `contextTokens` stays at its initialised 0. Measured before
  the run: 266/344 pi_compat sessions carry it, **0/11 claude-runtime and 0/16
  codex-runtime**. Those two arms therefore read `tokenUsage.inputTokens`, which
  `SessionManager` documents as "current context size (full conversation sent
  this turn), NOT accumulated" — the same fallback the renderer's own meter
  takes for them (`mayFallBackToTokenUsage`). A DIFFERENT counter from Pi's,
  named as such in every observation, never compared across arms.
- **`Session.contextUsage` at the DTO's top level depends on which door you
  ask.** `getSessions()` projects it on 0 of 383 sessions; `getSessionMessages(id)`
  returns it populated on the same session. Two projections of one field, and
  the cheap-looking read is the empty one.

### Rig facts this run added

- **A 60 000-character filler cannot be TYPED.** `typeText` dispatches three CDP
  events per character, so one filler turn would take about half an hour. The
  case pastes instead — `document.execCommand('insertText', false, text)`,
  page-side, one call, which dispatches `beforeinput`/`input` so the composer's
  React `onInput` runs. That is **not** the banned `Input.insertText`
  (`lib/connect.ts` `FORBIDDEN_METHODS`) and the ban's reason does not reach it:
  the ban exists because a slash command committed without key events never
  opens the palette and goes out as prose. This paste carries prose only, and
  the one command the case sends, `/compact`, still goes through
  `selectSlashCommand` and its palette assertion.
- **`getNessyGatewayJwt()` answers `{ jwt }`, not a string.** Passing the
  envelope straight into `setupLlmConnection` stores `[object Object]` as the
  credential; the handler's own message — `setup.credential?.includes is not a
  function` — points at the caller rather than at the shape.
- **A FAILED `setupLlmConnection` can still leave the connection behind.** The
  handler creates the record before it validates the credential, so a refused
  setup left `pi-api-key-91` on the profile and the next run correctly refused
  to touch it. A case that creates a connection must sweep on the failure path,
  not only on the success path.
- **Never `const window` in a page-side CDP expression.** It shadows the global
  for the whole function scope, so `window.electronAPI` on the first line throws
  `Cannot access 'window' before initialization` — a TDZ error that reads
  exactly like a preload that never finished.
- **30 s is not enough per CDP command for a big-transcript session.** A run
  that had already proven both halves died on
  `cdp-timeout: Runtime.evaluate did not answer in 30000ms`. Not IPC cost —
  `getSessions()` over 383 sessions measures 262 ms — but the renderer stalling
  under a quarter-million-character transcript while a turn lands. This case
  calls `runCase` with `timeoutMs: 180_000` instead of `main`.
- **The Claude SDK's transcript directory names its session.** `projects/<…>` is
  not a hash: the SDK slugifies the session's own cwd, which for a Nessy session
  is `<config>/sessions/<workspaceId>/<sessionId>`, so the directory name ENDS
  with the session id. `readClaudeSessionLog` now takes an optional `sessionId`
  and narrows to it — "newest across the workspace" is the ambiguity that once
  let a previous run's marker be read as this run's.
- **`getSessions()` does not hydrate `messages`; `getSessionMessages(id)` does.**
  Switching a turn-completion counter to the cheap-looking all-sessions door
  because the single-session door had timed out made the count 0 for every
  session, and the wait sat out its full ten minutes. The single-session door is
  the right one; the timeout is transient (it races a compaction) and the fix is
  a retry, not a different door.
- **`getStableMachineId()` is not stable, and the app calls the result
  corruption.** `secure-storage.ts` derives the credential key from
  `execSync('ioreg -rd1 -c IOPlatformExpertDevice | grep IOPlatformUUID')` and
  silently falls back to `username:homedir` when that command cannot run —
  `ioreg` lives in `/usr/sbin`, so a launch whose PATH lacks it derives a
  DIFFERENT v2 key. There is no migration between the two (only v1 → v2), so an
  app that once wrote credentials under one of them reports
  `Credential store is corrupt and was left untouched: decryption failed with
  all known keys` under the other, and every connection loses its credential.
  Hit live 2026-08-15 between two `electron:dev` launches of the same command.
  **Nothing was lost and the file was never damaged** — the product fails closed,
  as designed — and an out-of-band read confirmed all 11 entries decrypt with the
  fallback key. Recovery is to launch with the PATH the store was written under
  (`PATH` without `/usr/sbin`); the real fix is a product decision, not a rig one.

- **And the recipe for that PATH does not run on macOS as it is usually written.**
  `PATH=$(echo "$PATH" | tr ':' '\n' | grep -v '^/usr/sbin$' | paste -sd:)` — BSD
  `paste` requires a file operand, so it prints its usage line to stderr and the
  substitution yields an EMPTY PATH. The app then never starts and the shell
  reports the next command as "not found", which reads like a broken toolchain
  rather than a broken pipeline. Add the stdin operand: `paste -sd: -`. Cost one
  launch on 2026-08-15, and the failure is silent in a backgrounded pipeline
  whose output is filtered.

- **The whole `/usr/sbin` recipe is INERT on a packaged build, and that is not a
  detail — it is the difference between the workaround working and the app
  refusing every turn.** `apps/electron/src/main/shell-env.ts` `loadShellEnv()`
  runs at startup on macOS and **skips only when `VITE_DEV_SERVER_URL` is set**,
  i.e. only in dev. On the packaged `.app` it spawns `$SHELL -l -i -c env` and
  copies the login shell's whole environment over `process.env` — PATH included
  — so whatever PATH the launch supplied is gone before `getStableMachineId()`
  is ever called, `ioreg` resolves, and the v2-stable key is derived no matter
  what. Measured 2026-08-15 on packaged 1.26.0: launching WITH and WITHOUT
  `/usr/sbin` produced the same `Credential store is corrupt and was left
  untouched: decryption failed with all known keys`, and an out-of-band read
  (replicating `deriveStableKeyV2`/`deriveLegacyKeyV2` against
  `~/.nessy-agent/credentials.enc`) showed the store decrypts under the
  **`username:homedir` FALLBACK** id and under neither of the two the app tries
  — all 11 entries intact, revision 75, nothing damaged. The forward-migration
  covers v1→v2 only, so a store written by a `/usr/sbin`-less **dev** launch is
  permanently unreadable to a **packaged** one. The consequence for a live case
  is concrete: **a packaged run cannot use the operator's real profile on this
  machine.** Give it its own `NESSY_CONFIG_DIR` — boot once against an empty dir
  so the app writes its own `config.json`, then merge the connections you need
  into `llmConnections` and relaunch. Gateway cards need no credential store:
  they authenticate from `~/.nessy/dp_auth_creds.json`, which is outside the
  config dir and stays read-only to the harness.

- **The Claude SDK's project directory name is TRUNCATED, so
  `readClaudeSessionLog(…, sessionId)` can find nothing while the transcript is
  right there.** The lib narrows with `entry.endsWith(sessionId)` on the
  slugified working directory. Under a deep config dir the SDK truncates the
  slug and appends a short hash — session `05e2ccbe-1797-4d7e-b727-11d5caf0d4ed`
  filed under `…-05e2ccbe-1797-4d7e-b727-wwr101` — and the read returns
  `no-claude-transcript-for-session` about a runtime that wrote a full
  conversation. It survives under `~/.nessy-agent` and fails under a scratch
  path, which is the worst shape a narrowing can have. Fall back to the longest
  UNIQUE prefix of the session id (`session-tool-wire-parity.ts` `captureClaude`
  does), never to "newest across the workspace" — that is the ambiguity
  `lib/runtime-session-log.ts` documents as having let a previous run's marker
  be read as this run's.

### The guardrail, verbatim, and what it does and does not mean

A Claude-runtime turn against llm-proxy comes back:

```
API Error: 403 {"error":"failed to detect critical data: fail anon mask critical data:
anon error: details - {"error":"Security Guardrail Triggered: Malicious MCP detected.
Invalid tools: mcp__dpGitlab__dp_gitlab_create-merge-request, …,
mcp__plugin_figma_figma__download_assets, …, mcp__session__BashOutput,
mcp__session__TodoWrite, mcp__session__call_llm, …"}}
```

**403, not 401, and the body names the TOOL LIST** — including `mcp__session__*`,
which is Nessy's own built-in surface. A guardrail that parses the body and
enumerates its tools has already authenticated the request.

Two controls decide the reading, and both were run before any conclusion:

- **Gateway positive control** — the SAME runtime with the SAME tool surface on
  `claude-gateway`: `roles: ["user","assistant"]`, answer `GW-OK`, zero errors.
- **Wire asymmetry** — `BYO-QWEN` sends the SAME full tool surface (all three
  plugins enabled) over chat-completions to the SAME host with the same key and
  gets a clean answer. **The guardrail polices `/v1/messages` only.** Pi and
  Codex BYO on llm-proxy are unaffected; that is the blast radius, and it is the
  opposite of the worst case.

Deferred by owner decision 2026-08-15: llm-proxy admits only tools on its own
allowlist, and our `mcp__*` names are not on it. That is a platform-policy
conversation, not a code fix here.

### Two facts lane F will need

**The base context is ~70 000 tokens on a one-line prompt.** Measured from
Pi's own `/context` accounting on the owner's default connection, after a real
turn:

| category | tokens |
| --- | --- |
| `system_tools` | 20 182 |
| `mcp_tools` | 20 078 |
| `system_prompt` | 19 351 |
| `custom_subagents` | 8 622 |
| `skills` | 1 048 |
| `conversation_messages` | 603 |
| `memory_files` | 0 |
| unaccounted | 939 |
| **total** | **70 823** |

Tool schemas are the largest contributor but split evenly across two
categories — 40 260 together, 57%. The system prompt at 19 351 is the
surprise: nearly the size of either tool category on its own.

**The measurement must come AFTER a turn.** On a fresh session
`system_prompt` and `mcp_tools` both read 0 — they are not composed yet — and
the total looks like 28 415, understating the answer by 60%.

**So a 40 960-window model cannot answer at all on this profile.** Measured as
an endpoint refusal, verbatim: `400 {"message":"This model's maximum context
length is 40960 tokens. However, your request has 70042 input tokens…"}`. This
is not a compaction-threshold question — the first turn overflows before any
history exists — which makes the synced `contextWindow` a usability fact, not
a display detail. Carry it into F1's fabricated-window row.

### Rig facts this run added

- **`rg -rn 'pattern'` is `--replace n`, not `-r -n`.** ripgrep's `-r` takes an
  argument, so the `n` is consumed as the replacement text and every match is
  silently rewritten to the letter `n` in the output. It reads like a normal
  numbered grep. Measured 2026-08-15: it turned a generated schema's
  `"$ref": "#/definitions/TokenUsageBreakdown"` into
  `"$ref": "#/definitions/n"`, which was then read as the schema's actual
  content. It changed no conclusion only because the decisive evidence — the
  payload's field names — did not contain the search term. Line numbers come
  from `-n` alone.

- **A hash of nothing is still a hash.** A verification script guarded on
  `[ -z "$a" ]` after `a=$(git show … | shasum)` — but `shasum` of empty input
  returns `e3b0c44298fc1c14…`, so the guard never fired and the comparison ran
  against the digest of an empty string. It reported two intact files as
  DIFFERING while checking another session's stashed work, which is the worst
  possible moment for a false negative. Check the COMMAND's exit status, or that
  the byte count is non-zero — never that a derived digest is empty.

- **`open(p,'w').write(open(p).read().replace(...))` DESTROYS THE FILE.** Python
  truncates on `open(p,'w')` BEFORE evaluating the argument, so the read returns
  `""` and the write stores `""`. It exits 0 and the commit looks ordinary. This
  emptied the plan file once and this checklist once, in the same session, the
  second time in the very commit whose message explained the first. Always two
  statements: read, then write.

- **The SDK reports a 403 as `authentication_failed`**, and this repo's mapper
  turned that into *"Your API key may be invalid or expired"* — which sent an
  hour of work into hunting a credential bug that did not exist. Fixed: a 403
  now says the endpoint accepted the credentials and refused the request, and
  carries the upstream reason. The 401 wording is unchanged, with a positive
  control so the branch cannot widen.
- **`setSessionModel(sessionId, workspaceId, model)`** — three drivers passed
  the model into the `workspaceId` slot. The setter silently no-ops, and the
  session keeps whatever model it already had, so it *looked* correct on a
  single-model connection and produced two false FAILs elsewhere.
- **A plugin's top-level switch is not always labelled `Toggle <name>`.** Figma
  is an MCP-server plugin and its control reads **"Enable server"**; a script
  matching the former skips it silently and reports a plugin as untouched when
  it was never touched.

## Staged for the owner's check-in — lane G (rows PRE-FLIGHTED, not yet run)

Three drivers exist and have each been dry-run against the real profile on the
G1–G3 build. **Every one of them BLOCKS today, on purpose**: each is waiting for
a substrate only the owner can create, and each names that substrate in its own
blocked message rather than passing on whatever it happens to find. The verdict
column is what the sitting fills in.

| Case | Driver | Waiting on | Dry-run verdict, 2026-08-14 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `G3-WIRE` | `scripts/cdp/cases/g3-anthropic-wire.ts` | a `claude-runtime` connection holding a model stored with `wire: 'anthropic-messages'` — written only when `/v1/messages` really answers, which needs the entitled key | BLOCKED, correctly: the two `claude-runtime` connections on the profile carry `tgpt/*` and `openrouter/*` only |
| `BYO-CATALOG` | `scripts/cdp/cases/byo-model-catalog.ts` | a connection SAVED by this build | BLOCKED, correctly: `pi-api-key-2` holds four bare `claude-*` strings and `modelsSyncedAt: null` — the pre-lane-E shape |
| `BYO-QWEN` | `scripts/cdp/cases/byo-qwen-turn.ts` | a Qwen id on a BYO llm-proxy connection | BLOCKED, correctly: no BYO connection carries one |
| `COMPACT-LIFE` | `scripts/cdp/cases/compaction-lifecycle.ts` | permission to narrow ONE model's `contextWindow` in the owner's real `config.json` for the run (`NESSY_F5_ALLOW_CONFIG_WRITE=1`), plus a running app | BLOCKS on the permission gate by design. Substrate exists for all three arms: pi `pi-api-key-3`, claude `claude-gateway`, codex `codex-gateway`/`codex-custom` — every one carries an OBJECT model entry, which a bare-string entry could not. |

**RIG FACT — opening the connection EDIT form over CDP is flaky.** Roughly one
attempt in four succeeds. The Radix row menu opens, its items enumerate, `Edit`
receives the click and the menu closes — and no dialog mounts. No console error,
no exception, nothing in the DOM. It is the harness, not the product: the same
sequence works by hand, and it succeeded on the very next attempt more than once
with no code change in between. Measured 2026-08-15 across several sessions.

What helps, in the order that mattered:
- select the trigger by walking UP the DOM from the row's label to the ancestor
  that owns a `button[data-slot="dropdown-menu-trigger"]`. Nearest-by-y picked a
  NEIGHBOURING row's trigger after a reload shifted the layout;
- open the menu with dispatched `pointerdown`/`pointerup` — a bare `.click()` on
  the trigger does not open a Radix menu — then activate the item with a plain
  `.click()`. Dispatching pointer events on the ITEM instead swallows it;
- give the window a real `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` first after a reload;
- retry up to ~5 times and BLOCK with the reason rather than reporting a
  product failure.

A driver that needs the edit form should budget for this, and a driver that can
reach the same state through CREATE (or through the RPC directly, as
`BYO-CATALOG` does) should prefer that route.

**Why each blocks instead of running on what is there.** All three would
otherwise report a PASS that means nothing, and each has a specific vacuity:

- `G3-WIRE` — a connection whose models are all chat-completions turns happily
  and demonstrates no routing. The case additionally runs a NEGATIVE CONTROL
  before the turn: it probes the same model over chat-completions through the
  app's own RPC and REQUIRES that to fail. A model that cannot answer on
  chat-completions, answering in a live session, can only have gone through the
  passthrough — that is the discriminator, not a log line. And one wire working
  is not the claim, so the mixed half sends a second turn on a chat-completions
  model in the SAME session.
- `BYO-CATALOG` — a connection from an older build carries bare string ids and
  no stamp, so every check would FAIL and read as "the feature is broken" when
  the truth is "this connection predates it". BLOCKED means wrong substrate;
  FAIL means wrong behaviour; the guard keeps them apart.
- `BYO-QWEN` — this is the trap LIVE-PHASE HANDOFF #2 recorded. On a non-Qwen
  model there is no reasoning block to leak, so "no leak" is not an
  observation. The case also refuses to run its forced-dialect arm against a
  connection without the hint, or the unforced arm against one with it — either
  would measure one arm while claiming the other.

**`BYO-QWEN` half 2 got its instrument** (approved 2026-08-14). The plan
requires the `Authorization: Bearer` survival to be observed on the proxy's own
outgoing request, and reading `upstreamHeaders` in the source is explicitly not
that measurement — so `nestor-reasoning-proxy.ts` now writes one line per
request naming the SCHEME and never the value (`bearer` / `nestor-token` /
`none`), off unless `NESSY_PROXY_AUTH_DEBUG=1`. The case reads it from the
captured stderr and still BLOCKS if the log is absent, rather than passing on
half 1 alone. Run the app for this sitting as:

```
NESSY_PROXY_AUTH_DEBUG=1 NESSY_CDP_PORT=9224 bun run electron:dev 2>&1 \
  | grep -a 'nestor-proxy' > /tmp/nessy-proxy-auth.log
```

and pass `NESSY_QWEN_PROXY_LOG=/tmp/nessy-proxy-auth.log` to the case. The
filter keeps the capture to a handful of lines — an unfiltered `electron:dev`
redirect has filled this machine's disk twice.

Order at the sitting: form walk (owner types the key) → `BYO-CATALOG` →
restart → `BYO-CATALOG --after-restart` with the printed baseline → `G3-WIRE` →
`BYO-QWEN` (unforced arm, then the forced one).

Rig facts this run added: a case must read the session's own `error` rows
before attributing a failure to the tool it was driving — without that check
this run would have re-opened a fixed defect instead of finding a new one; and
a source's setup belongs to the DRIVER, not the model (three consecutive runs
were consumed by the model discovering the config schema, leaving a bare
directory, or sitting on an unanswered `file_write` prompt).

Logged, not chased: a `bypassPermissions` session (`41da7400`, mode confirmed
set, bypass acknowledged) still showed `pendingPermissions:[{type:"file_write",
source:"session-permission"}]` and a Write unanswered for a full 300 s window.
Not the protected-path floor — `protectedPathOutcome('bypassPermissions')`
returns `allow` and the floor is skipped entirely. Reproduced twice,
self-resolved once, uncharacterised; a candidate for its own task.

Cleanup: the four CDP fixture agent files from earlier waves are removed, the
probe source is gone, and the profile is back to the operator's 1 source, 3
plugins and 5 registries.

Rig facts this run added: the transcript reader returns an EMPTY `result` for an
MCP tool row — read the runtime's own session jsonl instead; and a case that
calls a tool with required arguments must supply them, or the failure it reports
is its own (`fileKey: must have required properties fileKey / Received
arguments: {}` was case incompleteness, not an auth refusal — the distinction is
the whole difference between a defect and a wasted owner click).

Process cost, recorded because it was avoidable: the owner's 3rd login click was
spent against a committed-but-NOT-rebuilt bundle. `electron:dev` does not pick
up a main-process change — AGENTS.md names this trap, and it still cost a click.

Debt: the regression test for `717a4e039` is not committed — the stub did not
match `OAuthPinnedFetch`'s signature, and a weakened test is worse than a named
gap. Carried as a Z1 task.

Rig facts this run added: `sendPrompt` rejects MULTI-LINE prompts
(`composer-mismatch` — the contenteditable does not round-trip `\n`), single
line only; `piRuntime()` has NO `tools` field (reading a tool surface through it
can only answer zero); `window.electronAPI.loadSourceConfig` does not exist —
read the on-disk config instead; a fixture registry needs a ROOT catalog
manifest (`marketplace.json`) or `addRegistry()` accepts it and the catalog
stays silently empty; a model may DECLINE a probe-shaped install prompt outright
(`{"status":"blocked","reason":"appears to be a probe or test"}`) — that
measures the model, so a case must re-prompt once with honest framing and report
BLOCKED-on-model, never FAIL.

## Run of 2026-08-13/14 — the codex agent-roles live gate, its fallout, and the auth-path split

Dev stand (`electron:dev`, bundles verified by value-symbols before every arm),
owner's real config dir (5 workspaces → `NESSY_CDP_WORKSPACE_ID` mandatory),
gateway `tgpt/qwen35-397b-a17b-fp8`.

| Case | Verdict | The one-line fact |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `CDX-ROLES` (new) | **PASS 16/16** | A codex session spawned `probe-role` via native `spawn_agent`; the child's developer message WAS the role file and its reply carried the fresh codeword; controls A/B returned `unknown agent_type` verbatim in codex's OWN rollout jsonl under `<CODEX_HOME>/sessions/`; the deny diagnostic named probe-denied's grant in the session log; the `agents.enabled` landmine did not fire; the parent called only collab tools. |
| `Q6-LIVE` (×3 on the final tree) | every claim ≥1 controlled live PASS | Catalog inversion PASS in all three runs (3 controls incl. the `nestor-cloud-agent` refusal); Pi model-fallback PASS (run 1); Claude registry + its positive control PASS (runs 2-3); the full claude-runtime chain — dispatch → model substitution announced → child ran → answer delivered via TaskOutput — proven by a late transcript read. The token-literal check stays red for a documented reason: the probe agent is a SECURITY persona and refused to parrot a planted token ("attempt to inject a fabricated finding"). Model-behaviour arms flake per run; the banked runs are `/tmp/q6-live-final{,2,3}.out` shapes recorded in the tracker. |
| `Q6-LIVE` dead-child arm | recipe no longer kills — arm re-scoped | The 261 946-char oversized-prompt fixture now FITS the gateway model's context: the child completes and answers. The arm banks the DELIVERY half live (completion notice arrived carrying the child's own output) and names the death half as a gap; the FAILED-shaped notice stays covered by the settling-path seam tests. Re-arming needs a recipe that still kills on this gateway. |
| `CDX-ROLES` fallout, fixed same night | 2 real defects | (1) A subagent thread's `turn/completed` finished the PARENT's turn — final report lost, child text in the parent transcript (`isForeignThreadNotification`, `c241944f9`). (2) Collab calls were invisible in chat — now `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent` tool rows, refused spawns close instead of spinning (`fe65067fd`). |
| `Q6-LIVE` fallout, fixed same night | 1 real defect | An agent pinning an unserved model (`claude-security` → `model: opus`) died on the gateway with "model not available" — now substituted with a logged announcement on `claude-runtime` connections; anthropic connections keep SDK alias pass-through (`9e9efbd54`, substitution announcements observed live in the session log). |
| `MCP-AUTH` (new) | pre-OAuth halves + dp PASS, then BLOCKED (owner OAuth) | figma (http + `oauth_resource`, app-driven OAuth): honest `needs-auth` from the app's own pending-auth summary; negative control — the same call does not succeed pre-login. dp half: with dp `ready` and `dpGitlab` enabled, `mcp__dpGitlab__dp_gitlab_whoami` materialized and answered with real corp data — the live positive of the needs-login-only withhold gate. BLOCKED names the one remaining step: the owner's browser OAuth, then re-run. |
| `ELC-1` | 30/32 (wave run) | Elicitation proven on all three runtimes: server received `accept` with the user's value, per-call nonce in the tool result, client capability read back by the server. The two reds: the model on Claude/Codex paraphrased instead of quoting the nonce — prose relay, not the feature. A retry rolled worse dice: the owner's **Guardian** denied the probe call as `credential_probing` before it reached the server (`pi-server-log: []`) — a rig/feature interaction to expect on probe-shaped prompts. |
| `ELC-2` | **PASS 28/28** | Non-http(s) URL elicitation refused with a single OK on all three runtimes; server received `decline`, confirmed in its own log. |

Standing rig facts from this run: the ELC fixture's own config dir NO LONGER
BOOTS the app (`custom-root-reset-required`, windowless) — run ELC against the
real profile with the probe merged into `~/.nessy-agent/mcp.json` (delete it
afterwards; it did not exist before); probe agents live in
`~/.nessy-agent/agents/` (`probe-role.md` needs a fresh codeword per scoring
run); a freshly launched app needs ~30 s before the first `freshSession()`.

## Re-run of 2026-08-13 — subagents across the runtimes, AFTER the four fixes

The same gate as the run below, re-driven at `31da9b13f` once `b97c774c0`,
`c086abed6`, `c87604aea` and `31da9b13f` had landed. Report and raw evidence:
`.superpowers/sdd/q8-regate-report.md`, `.superpowers/sdd/q8-regate-evidence.md`.
Packaged unsigned build of `31da9b13f` (the script reached its LAST statement —
`.build/upload/manifest.json` = `{"version":"1.25.1"}`), isolated
`NESSY_CONFIG_DIR=~/nessy-cdp-q8/config`, `claude-security` + `context7` from
`anthropics/claude-plugins-official` over the network, plus a seven-agent local
fixture. **Both shipped bundles were grepped for the fixes BEFORE any
measurement** (`PI_TOOL_PRESENTATION_REWRITES` ×3 and `modelSubstitution` ×5 in
`resources/pi-agent-server/index.js`; `buildClaudeRuntimeAgentOptions` ×2,
`runtimeDispatchesAgentDefinitions` ×4 in `dist/main.cjs`) — a dev run against a
stale Pi bundle would have tested the old logic.

| case | result |
| :-- | :-- |
| **`claude-security`'s OWN agents spawn, run and return (Pi)** | **PASS** — `scan-researcher` (`model: inherit`, `tools: … Agent(claude-security:explore)`, the exact Q6-D1 shape) returned `TAG=Q8TAG-7120B4B8` + `TOOL=bash/find/grep/read/subagent` in 49 s. The Q6 control pair re-run: `q8-agentbare` (`Read, Grep, Agent`) and `q8-control` (`Read, Grep`) in ONE parallel call both answer, only the first reports `subagent`. Parent has no `Read`/`Bash` row. **Q6-D1's manifest half CLOSED** |
| **a dead child says why (Q8-D1, sev-1)** | **FAIL** — parent still told `1. <agent> - done … (no output)` while the child's session holds one `[error] invalid_api_key` row and `piAgentViewOutcome: "completed"`. Break: invalidate the gateway credential through the app's own `setupLlmConnection`, then dispatch. Reproduced with two different agents. **Q6-D1's silent half is NOT closed** |
| the model fallback announces itself (Pi) | **PASS announcement / FAIL spawn** — `[model] scan-inventory asked for "sonnet", which this session cannot serve; it ran on "tgpt/qwen35-397b-a17b-fp8".` exactly as documented, then `runtime-agent-model-ceiling-mismatch` and the child never runs (`SessionManager.ts:11878-11881` compares the SUBSTITUTED model against the declared one). Same on the fixture, and for an invented model id — positive control on the reason |
| Claude runtime — a Nessy plugin agent is dispatchable | **FAIL** — `Agent type 'q8-control' not found. Available agents: claude, claude-code-guide, Explore, general-purpose, Plan, statusline-setup`, character-identical to the run below; same for `scan-researcher`. Positive controls in the same turns: the SDK's own `general-purpose` launched, and the plugin's MCP server was invoked |
| the catalog is honest about the active runtime | **FAIL** — `capabilities.getCatalog` returns 15 agents all `{"state":"active","reason":null}` for a Codex session, a Claude session, a Pi session AND the workspace, byte-identical. `resolveCapabilityContext` never sets `provider`, and the new gate treats `undefined` as "dispatches" by design |
| Codex runtime — a Nessy plugin agent is dispatchable | **GAP, unchanged** — `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent`/`send_input`/`close_agent`/`resume_agent` exist; no agent tool row in any attempt; the model's `CDXERR=OK` has no tool row behind it and is not counted |
| nested (depth-1) subagent spawn | **PASS, newly reachable** — `q8-agentbare` called `subagent` with `{"agent":"worker",…}` from inside a child; Q6 could never get here |
| foreground subagent tool timeout (Q6-D3) | **STILL PRESENT** — `get_subagent_result` timed out twice in one turn while both children finished; the parent then fabricated its answer from a `Bash` read |

**New traps this re-run paid for.** (1) **Bind and trust a project before
driving subagents.** With no project bound, every `Read`/`Grep` on an absolute
path raised an unanswerable `outside_root` approval and returned
`Tool execution timed out` — in the parent AND both children — which reads
exactly like a broken tool layer. `Bash` is not path-gated and worked, which is
what gave it away. (2) **Two CDP turn drivers cannot run at once**: the second
fails `composer-mismatch`, because the composer belongs to whichever session the
pane shows. (3) A 4.5 MB agent definition is silently dropped from the runtime
pool while the catalog still calls it `active` — an absent agent is not always a
registration bug.

## Run of 2026-08-13 — real subagent invocations on the three runtimes (pre-fix)

The layer Q5 did not reach: **spawning**, not tool-calling. Report and raw
evidence: `.superpowers/sdd/q6-agent-invocation-report.md`,
`.superpowers/sdd/q6-agent-invocation-evidence.md`. Packaged unsigned build of
`2f84d236b` (the script reached step 9 and produced the DMG — `EXIT=0`), isolated
`NESSY_CONFIG_DIR=~/nessy-cdp-q6/config`, `claude-security` + `context7`
installed from `anthropics/claude-plugins-official` over the network, plus a
seven-agent local fixture plugin isolating one variable per agent.

| case | result |
| :-- | :-- |
| a real installed plugin's agent spawns, runs and returns (Pi) | **PASS** — fixture `q6-plain` returned `TAG=Q6TAG-F31EED35` (token minted after launch, in a file no prompt quotes; the parent has no `read` row) plus exactly its declared ceiling `TOOL=read TOOL=grep` |
| the same, for `claude-security`'s OWN agents (Pi) | **FAIL** — `model: sonnet` agents are refused (`Agent model unavailable: sonnet`, Q6-D2) and every `model: inherit` agent dies on Q6-D1 |
| **a subagent that is itself granted `Agent` cannot start (Q6-D1, sev-1)** | **FAIL** — child session holds ONE row, `error: Pi execution manifest model contract mismatch for subagent`, stamped `piAgentViewOutcome: "completed"`; the parent is told `done … (no output)`. Controlled: `q6-agentbare` (`Read, Grep, Agent`) dies, `q6-control` (`Read, Grep`) answers, dispatched in one parallel call |
| no `runtime-agent-worktree-policy-mismatch` (Pi) | **PASS** — 0 matches in the app log and in every `session.jsonl` (`rg -a`); positive control, the same sweep finds `Agent model unavailable` in 2 files and the manifest-mismatch string in 4. Agents on both sides of the policy split spawned |
| a subagent granted `mcp__<server>__*` calls that server's tool (Pi) | **PASS** — the child's OWN session log lists exactly `mcp__plugin_context7_context7__query-docs`, `…__resolve-library-id`, `read` and carries Context7's real answer → `RESOLVED=/reactjs/react.dev` |
| a BARE `disallowed-tools: mcp__<server>` denies (Pi) | **PASS** — `q6-denied` got only `read` and said so; positive control `q6-mcp` (same grant, one line less) made the real call |
| the multi-argument `Agent(a, b, …)` grant survives | **PASS stored / FAIL functional** — `claude-security`'s six-way grant and the fixture's two-way grant are stored as ONE entry each, and the child really receives the tool — which is what kills it (Q6-D1) |
| Claude runtime — a Nessy plugin agent is dispatchable | **GAP** — `Agent type 'q6-plain' not found. Available agents: claude, claude-code-guide, Explore, general-purpose, Plan, statusline-setup`. The SDK's `Agent`/`TaskOutput` work with the SDK's own types; `claude-agent.ts` passes `plugins: []` and no `agents:` option |
| Codex runtime — a Nessy plugin agent is dispatchable | **GAP** — Codex carries its own `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent`/`close_agent`/`resume_agent`, and `config-writer.ts` writes no agents section at all. No agent tool row appeared in three attempts |
| subagent background default (depth 0, no explicit argument) | **PASS — matches the contract** — `PI CHILDREN: [… "background":true …]`; `NESSY_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS` absent from the app env. Every earlier `background:false` was the call site passing it explicitly |

**Traps this run paid for.** (1) A model self-report is worthless twice over
here: the Claude runtime denied having an `Agent` tool it demonstrably has, and
the Codex model produced a plausible `AGENTERR=unknown agent_type` with **no tool
row** behind it. Only transcript rows and on-disk child sessions were counted.
(2) **Bind a child to its agent by the child session's own `name` field**
(`"<agent>: <task…>"` in `<config>/sessions/<ws>/<childId>/session.jsonl`) — a
transcript that looked like `scan-researcher`'s was `scout`'s, and the whole
root-cause read would have been wrong. (3) A foreground subagent call often
exceeds the tool timeout while the child finishes fine (Q6-D3) — poll the child
sessions on disk before calling a dispatch broken. (4) A multi-line prompt fails
`composer-mismatch`; keep CDP prompts to one line, and avoid `...`.

## Run of 2026-08-12 — the 1.25.1 acceptance matrix on a packaged build

Acceptance gate for the 1.25.1 fix wave (`532d4470f`). Report and raw evidence:
`.superpowers/sdd/q5-acceptance-report.md`,
`.superpowers/sdd/q5-acceptance-evidence.md`.

**The artifact was UNSIGNED, by owner decision, and that bounds the gate.**
`build-dmg.sh arm64` with `CSC_IDENTITY_AUTO_DISCOVERY=false`. Two things a
future run must know. (1) The build aborts at
`⨯ cannot expand pattern "${env.NESSY_DESKTOP_UPDATE_URL}"` **before** the sign
step, and electron-builder has by then already written
`release/mac-arm64/Nessy Desktop.app` — an `.app` recovered from that failure is
`Signature=adhoc, TeamIdentifier=not set`. Driving it is how a run convinces
itself it measured a signed build. Set the README's local values
(`https://updates.invalid/dev`, `dev`). (2) With no identity electron-builder
**skips signing entirely** — it does not ad-hoc sign — so `mac.signIgnore` has
nothing to act on and the nested `codex-app-server` matches its pin trivially.
That is **not** evidence for the sev-1 fix and must never be recorded as one.
A Developer-ID build is not obtainable on this host either: the certificate lives
in a locked `nessy-desktop-signing.keychain-db` and signing dies with
`errSecInternalComponent`.

Isolated `NESSY_CONFIG_DIR=~/nessy-cdp-q5/config`; the operator's `~/.nessy-agent`
was never written and `~/.nessy/dp_auth_creds.json` was read only by the app,
through its own one-click card. All three connections were created that way
(`pi-api-key`, `codex-gateway`, `claude-gateway`), all `isAuthenticated`.

**GitHub egress on this host is degraded and it is not the product.**
`api.github.com` answers 403 with `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0` for the shared IP,
which is the only thing the default-branch lookup uses — hence
`plugin.install.repository_ref_unresolved` on every git marketplace.
`addRegistry({refName:'main'})` skips that call and `openai/plugins` loaded
immediately. Separately, codeload tarballs are intermittently killed
(`curl: (56) Connection died` on one repo while another returns 200 in the same
minute). The three upstreams were `git clone`d and added as LOCAL registries —
same bytes, different transport — while `context7` was still installed over the
real network so that path is proven too.

| case | result |
| :-- | :-- |
| all three runtimes complete a turn on the packaged build | **PASS** — Pi `PIBASE7731` 9 s, Claude `CLDBASE9027` 6 s, **Codex `CDXBASE4412` 4 s** (the sev-1's user-visible consequence) |
| **sev-2 — `claude-security` no longer kills Pi turns** | **PASS** — the real plugin (20 `allowed-tools`, six-way `Agent(...)`, 5 agents with parametrized grants) installed, Pi answered `PISEV2GREEN5589`; control `PIBASE7731` before it existed |
| the comma-bearing ceiling survives install | **PASS** — all **20** entries stored, `Agent(a, b, c, d, e, f)` intact as ONE entry (1.25.0 stored none) |
| `SKL-P1` metadata half | **PASS 12/12**. Activation half stays operator-run; not claimed |
| real forced MCP call × 3 runtimes | **PASS** — `context7/resolve-library-id`, unfakeable result `/reactjs/react.dev`. Names: `mcp__<wire>__<tool>` on Pi/Claude, `mcp__sources__<wire>__<tool>` on Codex |
| permission card names the real tool | **PASS** (Pi) — card carries both `mcp__plugin_context7_context7__resolve-library-id` and `plugin_context7_context7/resolve-library-id` |
| server-level MCP grant expands to that server's tools, and only that server's | **PASS** — `grantee` got context7's 2 tools + `read`, nothing from `t-tracker`(64)/`codex-security`(32)/`time`(2), and the call really returned |
| a BARE server-level deny denies | **PASS** — `denied`, same grant + `disallowed-tools: mcp__plugin_context7_context7`, got **only** `read`. Read at runtime from frontmatter (`runtime-agent-definition.ts:1023`), so its absence from `installed.json` is correct |
| marketplace count matches visible rows | **PASS** — 40 rendered cards vs "Showing 40 of 7509"; RPC keeps `visible`(749)=`items.length` apart from `total`(775) |
| stood-down capability explains itself | **PASS** — Codex app connectors `unsupported-runtime` + `plugin.app.connector_unresolved`; 26 unused dp servers `disabled`/`source-disabled` |
| `/compact` goes out bare | **PARTIAL (Pi)** — 0 added runtime entries, no echoed command, no context markers; the host answered `Nothing to compact (session too small)`, so the `compactions > 0` EFFECT half was not reached. **Codex: no instrument exists** (its `CODEX_HOME` is a temp dir removed on teardown) |
| skill `allowed-tools` ceiling refusal | **NOT MEASURED**, and **the case it asked for no longer exists**: since 2026-08-13 `allowed-tools` pre-approves and cannot refuse anything (owner-approved alignment; `2026-08-12-audit-defect-closure.md` deviations log). The measurement that replaces it: invoke the skill from the `/`-palette and check that a tool it LISTS runs with no permission card while a tool it does not list still shows one — and, for the restricting half, that a `disallowed-tools` entry is refused |

**RED/GREEN for the sev-1, on a copy** — the strongest signed-path evidence
obtainable without a certificate. `codesign --force --sign -` on the nested
binary reproduced the wave's own digits exactly
(`ac4fc97a…` 207 037 840 B → `6fda70f0…` 205 836 336 B, vendor identity and
hardened-runtime flag gone). `codex-integrity.ts` then **exit 1
`FAIL [sha-mismatch]`** on the tampered copy and **exit 0** on the healthy
`release/`. The tampered app's Codex turn produced no reply where the healthy one
answered in 4 s.

Three findings, all sev-3/4, all fail-soft: **(1)** a plugin-bundled hook that
references its own root cannot run — honey's `SessionStart` dies with
`Bash command blocked: contains ${} / $VAR syntax … "${NESSY_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/honey-session.js"`,
though that variable is the documented mechanism and we emit it ourselves;
**(2)** a mis-signed build refuses Codex **silently** — one user message, no error
row, no toast; **(3)** `DEBUG_LEVER` (`--debug`) is unusable on Electron 39.2.7 —
Node eats the flag (`[DEP0062]`) and no application log appears, so the note in
`scripts/cdp/lib/runtime-session-log.ts` is now wrong.

## Run of 2026-08-11 — `MCP-W1..W3`, the MCP wire-name gate, dev bundles AND a packaged build

The acceptance gate for `2026-08-11-mcp-followups-f2-f4.md` (G6). It closes the
four things every offline check on the 1.24.0 naming work left open: no live
network MCP call had ever happened, no model turn had ever invoked an MCP tool,
the packaged app had never been driven, and two marketplaces serving one plugin
name had never been reproduced.

Fixtures: `bun run scripts/cdp/fixtures/mcp-naming.ts` (see the recipe at the
end of this section), which builds under `NESSY_CDP_G6_ROOT` (default
`~/nessy-cdp-g6`). Isolated `NESSY_CONFIG_DIR=<root>/config`; the operator's
`~/.nessy-agent` was never written and `~/.nessy/dp_auth_creds.json` was read
only by the app. **Both fixture trees were deleted after the run** — rebuild
from the script, it is deterministic apart from its per-run tokens. Note the
builder's own trap: on a virgin dir it writes a config.json holding only
`builtinDpMcpServers`, and the app then starts, logs `App initialized`, and
never opens a window; boot once against an EMPTY config dir so the app writes
its own, then re-run the builder to merge. Dev bundles rebuilt first and CONFIRMED to carry the
code under test (`grep -ac` on `apps/electron/dist/main.cjs`:
`diagnoseUnusableMcpWireNames` 2, `sanitizeMcpNameSegment` 5,
`capability.dedup.builtin-identity` 1, `capability.mcp.unusable-wire-name` 1).

**"Packaged" here means the packaged `.app`, not a dmg.** The dmg step failed —
`⨯ cannot expand pattern "${env.NESSY_DESKTOP_UPDATE_URL}"`, the publish block
wants an env var a local build has no business supplying — but electron-builder
had already written `release/mac-arm64/Nessy Desktop.app`, and the `.app` is
what carries `isPackaged`. It was driven directly
(`…/Contents/MacOS/Nessy Desktop --remote-debugging-port=9225`), its renderer
loaded from `file:///…/Contents/Resources/app/dist/renderer/index.html`, and
`dist/interceptor.cjs` shipped inside the bundle. `release/` was deleted after.

**Re-measured the same evening after two changes landed** (dev only, no
packaged repeat): the product fix `4844f3a79` (the install-path normalizer now
keeps a skill's `allowed-tools`, the gap `MCP-W1`'s check 11 caught) and a
harness fix making `proxyToolName` runtime-aware. Dev bundles rebuilt from that
tree and the fix confirmed IN the bundle before anything was measured —
`apps/electron/dist/main.cjs:343093` reads
`const allowedTools = readStringArray(skillRecord.allowedTools) ?? readStringArray(skillRecord["allowed-tools"]);`
inside the skill branch, three lines below the `user-invocable` read. Fresh
fixture root, fresh `NESSY_CONFIG_DIR`, plugins re-installed through the app's
own marketplace RPC.

| case | dev | packaged |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `MCP-W1` | **PASS 11/11** (re-measured after `4844f3a79`; was 10/11) | 10/11 — pre-fix evidence, not re-run |
| `MCP-W2` Pi | **the tool call is green in every run**; the model self-report row is nondeterministic (final gate: `nonce=2d90822d9ce1`, `SKILL=YES`) | **PASS** — every check, pre-fix evidence |
| `MCP-W2` Claude runtime | **green in the final gate** (`nonce=9205df943d47`, `SKILL=YES`) — the earlier dev "managed Node runtime" failure did not reproduce | **the tool CALL passed**; the "model reports the fixture MCP tool among its own tools" check FAILED |
| `MCP-W2` Codex runtime | **the wave closes it**: `SKILL=YES`, the transcript row `mcp__sources__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami` exists, `nonce=a3ced445219a`, zero degradation lines — only the `TOOLS=NONE` self-report stays red | FAIL — pre-fix evidence, superseded by the dev re-runs |
| `MCP-W3` | **PASS 6/6** | **PASS 6/6** |

**The Claude runtime was deliberately NOT re-run.** Neither change can move it:
the dev build cannot start a plugin-bundled stdio server on that path at all
("managed Node runtime is missing", below), naming on the Claude runtime is
pool-shaped and untouched by the Codex-only `proxyToolName` fix, and the
`allowed-tools` fix is runtime-agnostic. Its packaged evidence stands as
recorded.

**Per-check split for `MCP-W2`, since "PASS" and "FAIL" are both too coarse
here.** Each runtime runs the same checks: the model's own `TOOLS=`/`SKILL=`
report (including the two F3 names), then the tool call itself.

Pi dev and Codex dev are TODAY's re-measurement; every other column is the
earlier run and is marked **pre-fix**. The name asked for is per-runtime:
`mcp__<wire>__<tool>` on Pi and Claude, `mcp__sources__<wire>__<tool>` on Codex
(see the Codex paragraph below).

| check | Pi dev (re-run) | Pi packaged (pre-fix) | Claude dev (pre-fix) | Claude packaged (pre-fix) | Codex dev (re-run) | Codex packaged (pre-fix) |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `listMcpTools` (independent read) | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| model lists the fixture MCP tool | ok | ok | ok | **FAIL** | FAIL (`TOOLS=NONE`) | FAIL |
| model lists the F3 sanitized name | ok — `mcp__my_server_v2__whoami` | — | — | — | FAIL | — |
| model lists the F3 hash-fallback name | ok — `mcp__server-ab518e32f3__whoami` | — | — | — | FAIL | — |
| model reports the bundled skill | ok | ok | ok | ok | FAIL (`NO`) | FAIL (`NO`) |
| app dispatched the minted wire name | ok | ok | FAIL | ok | FAIL — unmeasurable on Codex, see below | FAIL |
| nonce came back through the tool result | ok — `nonce=9e6881ae2893` | ok — `nonce=dd46133bd1a7` | FAIL | ok — `nonce=5013aef24a9a` | FAIL — same reason | FAIL |
| the reply carries the nonce | ok | ok | FAIL | ok | FAIL as a CHECK — but the reply really does carry one, see below | FAIL |

**Correction to an earlier version of this table.** The Codex "reply carries the
nonce" cell was recorded as `ok` after the `90ca1e4fe` run. That was wrong about
the CHECK: it is `nonce && answer.includes(nonce)` where `nonce` is read off the
transcript's tool row, and Codex writes no tool row — so the check was red then
and is red now. What was right is the underlying fact, which has to be read off
the `codex-answer` observation instead: the reply carries a fresh per-call nonce
on every post-fix run (`3838e8c49e87`, then `da1f7dff16f7`). The check cannot
see it; the observation can.

Pi dev is **9/9** on the re-run — the regression control for both changes, and
it stayed green.

**Read the Claude-packaged row carefully: the model did NOT enumerate the plugin
tool when asked, and then called it successfully.** Treat that cell as "the
model did not enumerate it when asked", never as "the tool was absent" — a
model's self-report is not an inventory, and the independent `listMcpTools` read
answered `ok` for the same capability in the same run.

The two F3-name checks (`my_server_v2`, `server-<10 hex>`) were added by the
review follow-up and are measured on the dev arms only; the packaged columns
predate them — the follow-up's other change (keying the nonce read on
`toolName === <the minted name>` and on THIS turn) is provably equivalent on the
evidence already recorded, because each passing arm's `*-mcp-tool-calls`
observation holds exactly one entry and it is that tool.

**A hidden window silently eats every keystroke, and the whole first re-run was
void because of it.** `document.hidden: true` (the app behind another window,
no `--disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows`): `focusComposer` succeeded,
`document.activeElement` WAS the composer, and the typed characters still never
arrived — `sendPrompt` threw `composer-mismatch: … composer holds "\n"` on every
turn of both arms, so nine checks failed for a runtime that was never asked
anything. `osascript -e 'tell application "System Events" to set frontmost of
(first process whose name contains "Electron") to true'` fixed it and the same
run went green. **Check `document.hidden` before believing a red MCP-W2 arm.**

**The model's self-report is nondeterministic, and the table's `model lists…`
rows must be read as such.** The first re-run of the Pi arm answered the same
prompt in PROSE — no `TOOLS=` line at all — so all three self-report checks
failed while the tool call in the same run passed (`nonce=a35be4e3dc62`). The
second re-run emitted the full list and all three passed. Four Pi turns were
observed in total and three produced the list. A red `model lists…` cell is
therefore weak evidence on its own; the paired `listMcpTools` row and the tool
call are the instruments.

**The mint agrees with what a model sees.** The Pi model's own tool list, live:
`mcp__my_server_v2__whoami, mcp__plugin_cdp-dual-probe_dual__whoami,
mcp__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami, mcp__server-ab518e32f3__whoami,
mcp__session__…, mcp__t-tracker__dp_tracker_…` — F3's sanitize (`my server.v2`
→ `my_server_v2`), F3's all-disallowed fallback (`мой сервер` →
`server-ab518e32f3`), the plugin-qualified form, and the built-in's untouched
bare name, all four in one list. This is the ONLY surface where a user can read
a minted wire name (G5 deviation log), so it is also the only place the mint can
be checked against what a permission rule would have to be written against.

**A real tool call, on the shipped artifact.** `mcp__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami`
dispatched, `toolStatus: completed`, result `plugin-echo G6PLUGIN-… nonce=dd46133bd1a7`,
and the model's reply carried the same nonce. The nonce is minted inside the
server process per call and exists in no file, which is what separates "the tool
ran" from "the model read the token off disk".

**CLOSED — `MCP-W1`'s one failing check, and what it cost to find.** The gate
found `normalizePluginCapability`'s skill branch rebuilding the capability
without `skill.allowedTools`, and `management-service-v2.ts:857` running it on
every install — so `summary.skillAllowedTools` was always undefined for an
installed plugin and G1's `safeArgs.inertComponents` list was always empty in
the real app, whatever the unit tests said. Fixed in `4844f3a79` and
re-measured here on a fresh install: the stored manifest now reads
`{"slug":"cdp-tracker-helper","path":"skills/cdp-tracker-helper/SKILL.md","allowedTools":["mcp__t-tracker__*"]}`,
the dedup diagnostic carries `safeArgs.inertComponents = skill:cdp-tracker-helper`,
and the server's detail page renders the whole sentence a user reads:
**"Duplicates a built-in server; the built-in is used. Parts of this plugin that
used this server are inactive: skill:cdp-tracker-helper"**. `MCP-W1` is 11/11.
The invocation-time ceiling was never affected — it reads SKILL.md itself, which
is why a live refusal quoting `outside the invoked skill tool ceiling` was
observed while the manifest copy was missing.

**Open, and now NAMED — the Codex runtime rejects every MCP tool call, and the
earlier "never sees plugin MCP tools" reading was half detector defect.** Two
distinct things were tangled, and the re-run separates them:

- **The name was the harness's fault.** Codex consumes the whole pool through
  one aggregating HTTP bridge declared as `[mcp_servers.sources]`; the bridge
  strips `mcp__` and codex-app-server re-prefixes with its own server key, so
  the real name is `mcp__sources__<wire>__<tool>`
  (`packages/shared/src/mcp/pool-server.ts:205-208`). The first run forced Pi's
  shape, and `Unknown tool` was the correct answer to a wrong question.
- **Asking for the right name exposes a REAL product gap, one layer deeper.**
  With `mcp__sources__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami` the runtime no longer
  says "Unknown tool" — it recognises the tool, tries to call it, and the turn
  comes back `TOOL-FAILED: user rejected MCP tool call` although nothing ever
  asked a user (`awaitingApprovalCount` stayed 0 and the case's approver clicked
  nothing). The app's own log names the cause:

```
[app-server stderr] ERROR codex_app_server::bespoke_event_handling:
failed to deserialize McpServerElicitationRequestResponse: missing field `action`
```

  `McpServerElicitationRequestResponse.json` requires `action` ∈
  `accept|decline|cancel`. `codex-agent.ts:359` answers `{}` to any method
  `isApprovalMethod` does not know, and that list
  (`packages/codex-agent-server/src/approval-bridge.ts:75-81`) carries
  `item/commandExecution/requestApproval`, `item/fileChange/requestApproval` and
  `item/permissions/requestApproval` — **not** `mcpServer/elicitation/request`.
  So the response fails to deserialize and the call is treated as rejected.

  Scope, stated carefully: this is measured on ONE tool, on dev. It is not a
  naming defect — the mint is fine and the corrected name reaches the runtime —
  and NO product code was changed for it here. `[McpPoolServer] Listening on
  127.0.0.1:63115` is the only pool-server line the run produced; there is no
  evidence of a `tools/list` ever being served to Codex, which is consistent
  with the model answering `TOOLS=NONE`, and that half stays unexplained.

**CLOSED — the Codex tool call round-trips once its approval is answered
(`90ca1e4fe`, re-measured the same evening on dev).** The bridge now recognises
`mcpServer/elicitation/request`; the bundle was confirmed to carry it before
measuring (`apps/electron/dist/main.cjs:427857` — `isApprovalMethod` ends
`|| method === MCP_ELICITATION_METHOD`, and `buildElicitationContent` is at
`:427837`). One codex-only run of `MCP-W2`, `bypassPermissions`, and the whole
chain is in the app's own log in eight seconds:

```
20:52:03.807 Message: Call the tool mcp__sources__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami …
20:52:05.700 Permission request for session 0910bfd5-…: undefined
20:52:10.967 Permission response for 7ffb91fc-…: allowed=true, alwaysAllow=false
20:52:10.973 [McpPoolServer] Tool call: plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami → mcp__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami
```

and the model's reply is `plugin-echo G6PLUGIN-A23DA053D3F4 nonce=3838e8c49e87`.
**Zero `failed to deserialize` lines in the whole run** (the pre-fix signature,
searched for explicitly). The pool-server line is the independent half: the
bridge-stripped name codex sends maps back onto the mint, so the naming contract
holds end to end through the `[mcp_servers.sources]` bridge — and the nonce is
minted per call inside the server process and exists in no file, so the reply
cannot have been fabricated.

Three things the same run leaves open, none of them this fix's job:

- **The transcript records no tool row on the Codex path**, so `MCP-W2`'s
  "app dispatched …" and "nonce came back through the tool RESULT" checks are
  structurally unmeasurable there and stay red — `codex-mcp-tool-calls: []`
  against a call the pool server logged. The same absence shows in the
  permission line above: the card's subject renders `undefined` rather than the
  tool's name, so a user is asked to approve something unnamed. Small,
  separate, observability-shaped.
- **`TOOLS=NONE` persists** — the model still enumerates no MCP tools in the
  turn BEFORE the one where it successfully calls one. The `tools/list` half
  remains unexplained, and this run makes it sharper: self-report and capability
  disagree within one session.
- **`SKILL=NO` persists** (`YES` on Pi and the Claude runtime). Recorded as the
  smaller skills-on-Codex gap, unchanged by this fix.

A permission card appeared even under `bypassPermissions`
(`codex-permissions-approved-mid-turn: 1`), exactly as on Pi — the mode does not
suppress an MCP approval on either runtime.

**Re-measured again after the two hardening commits (`290546a20` strictness +
`786fecd37` degradation logging), dev, codex arm only.** The risk being tested
was specific: if codex's real `mcp_tool_call_approval` form carried a required
property beyond `approval_mode`, the new "accept only when every `required`
field is filled" rule would silently turn the allow into a decline. It does not.

- Bundle verified first — `apps/electron/dist/main.cjs:428676` carries
  `` `codex elicitation allow degraded to decline: ${reason}` ``.
- **Zero `allow degraded to decline` lines in the entire run**, searched for
  explicitly. The accept survived the strictness.
- The tool still round-trips: reply
  `plugin-echo G6PLUGIN-F34537546D1E nonce=da1f7dff16f7`, and the pool server
  logged `Tool call: plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami → mcp__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami`
  0.003 s after `Permission response … allowed=true, alwaysAllow=false`.
- **The card names what it is asking about now.** Read off the DOM while it was
  up (a watcher polling `[data-tutorial="permission-banner"]` beside the run,
  because the case's approver answers it within ~2 s):

```
Permission required
Tool: sources
Allow the sources MCP server to run tool "plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami"?
sources          [Allow] [Deny]
This approval applies to this one connection and cannot be remembered
```

  Three things that were previously absent or wrong are visible in that one
  card: the tool's own name (the pre-fix card, and the log line behind it, said
  `undefined`), the server it belongs to, and the `noAlwaysAllow` posture spelled
  out in words. The matching log line now reads `Permission request for session
  …: sources` where it used to read `…: undefined`.

Unchanged by either hardening commit, still open: no tool row in the Codex
transcript (so two checks stay structurally red), `TOOLS=NONE`, and `SKILL=NO`.

## Final gate of the wave — `MCP-W2` full matrix on dev, HEAD `ee3493054`

Seven more commits landed (codex receives the PromptBuilder context parts;
`mcpToolCall` items become transcript rows; session commands go out bare).
Bundle verified first: `buildSkillListingPart()` emitting `<available_skills>`
at `apps/electron/dist/main.cjs:398004-398008`, and the row mapper
`mcpRowToolName → mcp__${item.server}__${item.tool}` at `:428243`. Fresh fixture
root, fresh config dir, all three arms in ONE run. **`MCP-W1` was NOT re-run —
nothing in this wave touches the dedup/inert-components surface it measures; its
11/11 from the `4844f3a79` re-measure stands.**

| check | Pi | Claude runtime | Codex |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| model lists the fixture MCP tool | FAIL (self-report, see below) | FAIL (lists only `mcp__session__*`) | FAIL (`TOOLS=NONE`) |
| model lists the F3 sanitized / hash names | FAIL (same turn) | FAIL | FAIL |
| **model reports the bundled skill** | **ok `SKILL=YES`** | **ok `SKILL=YES`** | **ok `SKILL=YES`** |
| **app dispatched the minted wire name** | **ok** `mcp__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami` | **ok** same | **ok** `mcp__sources__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami` |
| **nonce through the tool RESULT** | **ok `2d90822d9ce1`** | **ok `9205df943d47`** | **ok `a3ced445219a`** |
| **the reply carries the nonce** | **ok** | **ok** | **ok** |

**The three things this wave set out to prove, measured:**

- **Skills ride a Codex turn.** `SKILL=YES` on Codex, where every previous run
  answered `NO`. The PromptBuilder context parts reach that runtime now.
- **A Codex MCP call is a transcript row.** The two checks that were
  *structurally* red — "app dispatched …" and "nonce through the tool RESULT" —
  both flipped, because the transcript now carries
  `{tool: "mcp__sources__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami", status: "completed"}`
  with the result text behind it. Nothing about the case changed; the surface
  it reads appeared. **Not observed:** the row's display meta resolving the real
  server behind the `sources` bridge (`4a2b38d67`) — `getSessionMessages` did not
  project `toolDisplayName`/`toolDisplayMeta` in this read and the content pane
  would not render for a DOM read, so that half is unverified here, not refuted.
- **A session command goes out bare.** `/compact` on the Codex session, driven
  through the palette, logs exactly `[session] Message: /compact` — no context
  preamble, and `<available_skills>` appears nowhere in any logged outgoing text
  in the run.

  **This bullet's INSTRUMENT does not exist on a packaged build, so the claim is
  dev-only evidence — it is not retracted, it is unrepeatable as written.**
  `apps/electron/src/main/logger.ts` disables both electron-log transports
  whenever `isDebugMode` is false, which on a packaged Electron build it always
  is; the independent audit of the signed 1.25.0 searched for the line and found
  only `<config>/logs/auto-update.log`. And the line prints the RAW incoming
  `message`, not the `effectiveMessage` that reaches `agent.chat()` — what
  actually carried the claim was the ABSENCE of context markers in the other
  logged outgoing text, which is a weaker thing than the sentence above sounds.
  `CMP-N6` replaces it with an instrument that survives on the shipped artifact
  (the runtime's own session file, via `scripts/cdp/lib/runtime-session-log.ts`)
  — and note that this run's arm was **Codex**, the one runtime for which that
  instrument does not exist either, because its `CODEX_HOME` is a temp dir the
  agent removes on teardown.

Zero `allow degraded to decline` lines in the whole run: the hardened accept
still accepts.

**The Claude runtime's dev tool call now succeeds.** The earlier dev failure
("managed Node runtime is missing") did not reproduce — `nonce=9205df943d47`,
and no approval was needed (`approvals: 0`). Recorded as a fact, not a cause:
this run launched Electron DIRECTLY (`node_modules/electron/dist/…/Electron
apps/electron --remote-debugging-port=9225 --disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows
--disable-renderer-backgrounding`) against a standalone `vite dev`, where the
earlier runs used `bun run electron:dev`. Both are dev builds; which of the two
differences moved it is unmeasured.

**Why that launch shape was necessary, and it is a rig note worth keeping.** The
app's window never became visible on this host (`document.hidden: true`,
`document.hasFocus(): false`, and System Events reporting *"Can't get window 1 of
process Electron"* — an unattended machine near 01:40). Every keystroke is
dropped in that state, which is the `composer-mismatch` void described above.
`caffeinate`, `Page.bringToFront` and AXRaise all failed to fix it; the two
Chromium flags did — `document.hidden` went false and typing landed on the first
try. **If a CDP run's turns all fail with an empty composer, launch Electron with
those flags before believing anything the run says.**

**The `model lists…` row failed on ALL THREE arms this time, and that row keeps
earning its reputation as the weakest instrument in the table.** On Pi the model
did not answer the question at all — it INVOKED `cdp-tracker-helper` and replied
with the skill's own output ("The `cdp-tracker-helper` skill has been
invoked…"), despite the prompt's explicit "do NOT invoke, run or load that
skill". On Claude it listed only `mcp__session__*`. On Codex it said
`TOOLS=NONE` — in the same session where it then called the tool successfully.
Every arm's independent `listMcpTools` read answered `ok`, and every arm's tool
call worked. Self-report and capability disagree, and capability is the one that
was measured.

**Also open, smaller — the plugin's bundled SKILL is `NO` on Codex** (`YES` on
Pi and on the Claude runtime, both builds). Recorded, not diagnosed, and not
fixed: skills-on-Codex is its own question, and the Codex model's self-report is
the weakest instrument in this table (it answered `TOOLS=NONE` in the same turn
where the runtime demonstrably resolved a tool).

**A dev-build verdict was retracted by the packaged run — read this before
trusting a dev-only MCP result.** On dev, the Claude runtime logged
`Plugin MCP plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo is unavailable; managed Node runtime is
missing` at session start and the call failed. In the PACKAGED build the same
call succeeds (`nonce=5013aef24a9a`). `resolvePluginNodeRuntime` prefers a
bundled runtime file only a packaged app has, so plugin-bundled stdio MCP
servers are a dev-build limitation on that path, not a product defect.

**Four traps this run paid for**, each written out here rather than referenced —
the run's own working notes lived in a controller-session scratch file that is
NOT in the repo, so anything that matters has to survive in this section. An MCP
server's `env` values are credentials and the runtime REDACTS them out of
tool output (`[REDACTED] [REDACTED]` where the probe answered) — bake fixture
identity into the script instead; `declaredEndpointIdentity` is
`stdio:[command, ...args]:<env fingerprint>`, so two unrelated fixture plugins
with the same command line correctly deduped each other; a hashbang below byte 0
is a `SyntaxError` and made every fixture server read as `refused: failed`; and
EVERY MCP tool call is classified `mcp_mutation` and raises a permission request
that `bypassPermissions` does not suppress — a turn parked on it never ends, so
a case's own turn timeout cannot save it.

Fixture recipe (four calls after `bun run scripts/cdp/fixtures/mcp-naming.ts`
and a first app start, all through the app's own RPC):
`marketplace.addRegistry({source: '<root>/market-a'})` and `…/market-b`, then
`marketplace.install({source:{kind:'listing', marketplaceId, listingId: '<marketplaceId>:cdp-echo-probe', listingKind:'plugins'}})`
for `cdp-echo-probe` + `cdp-tracker-probe` from A and `cdp-dual-probe` from BOTH.
Connections come from the app's own one-click gateway path
(`ensureGatewayAuth()` → `getNessyGatewayJwt()` → `setupLlmConnection` for
`pi-api-key`, `claude-gateway`, `codex-gateway`) — the JWT never leaves the page
context. **Reload the window after creating them**: the wizard does not exit on
its own, and a route dispatched while onboarding is up reads the wizard's text.

## Run of 2026-08-08 (evening) — `AGT-9`, auto-compaction headroom, dev + rebuilt Pi bundle

New case, and the first live measurement of the auto-compaction threshold. Run
on `electron:dev` rather than a packaged build, deliberately and for a stated
reason: the arithmetic under test lives in the Pi bundle, dev loads it from
`packages/pi-agent-server/dist/index.js` (`runtime-resolver.ts`
`resolveServerPath`, not-packaged branch), and that bundle was rebuilt from the
fix first. The packaged-build rule exists for the interceptor, which this
measurement does not touch. Real gateway, real JWT.

| check | result |
| :-- | :-- |
| headroom on an ordinary session | **PASS** — window 262 144, threshold 60 000, reserve 202 144, `threshold + 25 000 < window`. The reserve is exactly what the fix computes (`0acdd4511`) |
| auto-compaction enabled | **PASS** — `autoCompactionEnabled: true`, `source: sdk_usage` |
| a real crossing, then compaction | **PASS** — with `NESSY_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW=90000` the session crossed 60 000 at turn 9 and compaction fired repeatedly: **7 compaction entries** in the session's own `.pi-sessions/*.jsonl` (`8e1b3f91`). Growth fell from **1 566 tokens/turn before the crossing to 181/turn after** — the context plateaus rather than running away. A second, longer session measured the same shape with 9 compactions |
| the turn after a compaction | **PASS** — completes, no 400; context stayed inside the window throughout |
| app's meter vs the gateway's own count | **12 of 12 turns identical, 0.0000 % divergence** — the meter is anchored on the provider's reported total, which is also what the threshold is compared against. This is the measurement that closes the "estimator drift" question in the 429 plan's deferred list: there is no drift to defend against on the healthy path |

**Four detectors, three of them wrong — the case's history is the finding.** Each
looked authoritative and each reported a product defect that was not there:
`ContextUsageSnapshot.lastCompaction` (declared in the type, never filled — it
reported "never compacted" through twenty-seven real compactions); a transcript
service-message match, AGT-6's method (matched a message left by a PREVIOUS run
in a session the harness had fallen back to); `TokenUsage.contextTokens` (a
different counter from the `inputTokens` the threshold uses). The surviving
detector is the one the product actually promises — growth stops. Ground truth
throughout came from the session's own Pi journal, not from a renderer field.

**The narrowing knob has a floor.** `NESSY_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW=30000` was tried
first and manufactured its own failure: a session's base context is ~46 000, so
the threshold sat below anything the session could reach, it compacted after
every turn forever, and the numbers read exactly like "compaction is broken".
Use 90 000.

**The plateau does not begin at the threshold, and a case that assumes it does
cuts its own run short.** Compaction keeps the recent tail (`keepRecentTokens`,
20 000 as applied) on top of the base context, so the steady state settles ABOVE
the threshold — ~66 000 for a 60 000 threshold, about five turns after the
crossing. AGT-9 therefore runs until the plateau is measurable rather than for a
fixed number of turns; an earlier fixed window of five stopped while the session
was still climbing and scored a working product as failing.
