An over-broad sweep would take live task lists and nothing in this checklist would notice | a scratch `NESSY_CONFIG_DIR` with two seeded task lists, one back-dated past the cutoff and one current |

### Managed policy (admin)

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ |  | `AGT-8a` | A managed model allowlist refuses VISIBLY: selecting a model outside `availableModels` is rejected with `MODEL_RESTRICTED_BY_POLICY`, the session stays on its current model, and the picker raises "This model is restricted by your organization's managed policy" with the `{{model}} is not on the allowed model list` description. Drive it through the MODEL PICKER: the case's RPC call bypasses the handler that raises the toast, so the visible half is only captured opportunistically otherwise | managed policy at the host path (`/Library/Application Support/Nessy/managed-policy.json` on macOS, `/etc/nessy/…`, `%ProgramData%\Nessy\…`) whose `availableModels` omits one model, plus `NESSY_CDP_BLOCKED_MODEL=<that model>`; needs sudo — see the fixture recipe below |
| ⬜ |  | `AGT-8b` | Withdrawing the managed policy restores the FULL model list — the data-loss guard: enforcement must never have written a narrowed list back into `config.json`. Operator-run by design; the harness deliberately does not write managed configuration, which is why this half is permanently BLOCKED for the automated case | the same policy file, then removed, and the app relaunched |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `ADM-N1` | **The consent gate for server-delivered settings.** Deploy a managed policy carrying an `env` block and a hook, then launch: a native modal titled "Approve managed settings" appears BEFORE the first window, listing field names, categories and counts and NO values (a managed `env` block routinely carries an API key), with buttons **Approve** and **Reject and quit** — and rejecting must QUIT the app. The whole Managed policy section is otherwise one model-allowlist row, and this is the highest-stakes admin surface in the product | a managed policy file at the host path carrying an `env` block and a hook; sudo, as `AGT-8a` |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `ADM-N2` | **The version floor, in both directions.** Set managed `requiredMinimumVersion` ABOVE the installed build and launch: the app refuses to start with a message telling the user to update through the organization's approved method. Then set it to garbage (`"latest"`) and launch again: the app starts NORMALLY (the bound is stripped, fail-open by design). Both failure directions are catastrophic and opposite — a fleet that cannot start, or a floor that silently does nothing | the managed policy file, edited between two launches |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `ADM-N3` | **A settings-refresh gate must not lock the user out.** With managed `forceRemoteSettingsRefresh` on, launch while SIGNED OUT of the gateway: the app must route to the one-click sign-in and re-run the gate rather than exit. Then launch signed IN but with the settings endpoint unreachable: it must block with a recovery message. The `needs-authentication` / `blocked` split is a deliberate deviation from the upstream rule — a literal port makes expired credentials unrecoverable, because the fetch needs a JWT, the JWT needs a sign-in, and the sign-in lives in the process that just exited | the managed policy key, plus a signed-out state for the first arm and an unreachable settings endpoint (hosts entry) for the second |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `ADM-N4` | **A disabled permission mode must be unreachable, not merely unlisted.** Deploy a managed policy with `disabledPermissionModes: ["bypassPermissions","auto"]`, then try to reach those modes from the composer's mode selector AND via Shift+Tab cycling, and start a turn: the session must end up in the allowed fallback and never RUN in a disabled mode. Prove the consequence, not the list — the renderer's cycle list is seeded from workspace settings rather than from the policy, so a mode missing from the picker is not evidence and a mode present in it is not a defect on its own | the managed policy key; a session whose workspace settings enable all modes |

### Hooks, skills & plugin updates — the wave's last units (2026-08-07)

Added when R64, R69, R73, R75 and R81/R82 landed. Each row proves the
USER-VISIBLE consequence; the unit tests already pin the contracts.

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ | 🔴 | `HOK-N1` | **Two hooks on ONE event no longer erase each other.** This was live before R64, with no new field involved. Register two `WorktreeCreate` hooks that each return a different `worktreePath`: exactly one path must win and the worktree must be created AT it — the pre-fix behaviour was no path at all. Then register an `McpElicitation` hook returning `{"decision":"accept","content":{…}}` alongside a second hook returning only `additionalContext`: the accept AND its values must both survive, and the dialog must NOT open. A merged result that keeps the context and drops the accept is the exact defect. | two probe plugins, each declaring one hook on the same event |
| ⬜ | 🔴 | `HOK-N2` | **A `SessionStart` hook can rename the session, and the rename sticks.** Emit `{"sessionTitle":"auth-refactor"}` and confirm the session is renamed in the sidebar — then send enough turns that the automatic title generator runs, and confirm it does NOT overwrite the hook's name. Repeat with a manual `/rename`: the generated title must not overwrite that either. | one probe plugin with a `SessionStart` command hook |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `HOK-N3` | **A `SessionStart` hook can force a skills reload, and it lands before the turn uses skills.** Add a new SKILL.md to a project skills dir WHILE a session is open, then start a turn whose hook returns `{"reloadSkills":true}` — the new skill must be invocable in that same turn, not the next one. | one probe plugin; a project skills directory writable mid-session |
| ⬜ | 🔴 | `HOK-N4` | **A `Setup` hook is refused with a reason, never silently dead.** Import a Claude plugin bundle declaring a `Setup` hook: the plugin's report must show the deferral naming `SessionStart` as the replacement. Repeat via `automations.json` (a WARNING that names the reason, and the rest of the file must still load), and via the `automation_management` tool (an error naming the replacement). The failure to catch is any surface that ACCEPTS it — a hook that registers and never runs is what this unit exists to prevent. | a Claude plugin bundle with a `Setup` hook; an `automations.json` carrying one alongside at least one valid automation |
| ⬜ | 🔴 | `SKL-N3` | **A skill's `!` shell placeholders actually run.** Write a skill whose body contains an inline `` !`git status --short` `` and invoke it: the model must receive the command's OUTPUT, not the literal placeholder. Confirm the Bash permission prompt appears once per placeholder. Then confirm the `allowed-tools: Bash(gh *)` case that used to be impossible: a skill declaring it, with a `` !`gh --version` `` placeholder, must match the rule and run WITHOUT a prompt — backticks reaching the shell as command substitution is what used to make that rule unmatchable. | a project skill with shell placeholders; `gh` on PATH |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `SKL-N4` | **Placeholders run on the FORKED-subagent route too**, where they never ran at all before. Give a `context: fork` skill a placeholder and invoke it; the child's prompt must carry the substituted text. Also confirm a DENIED subagent never runs the skill's shell — the rendering happens after the admission decision. | a `context: fork` skill with one placeholder |
| ⬜ | 🔴 | `CMD-N4` | **A skill and a command of the same name produce ONE row, and it runs the skill.** Put `review.md` in the project commands dir and a `review` skill in the skills dir, then open the palette and type `/review`: exactly one row, and running it must run the SKILL. Two identically-labelled rows is the specific failure — before the fix the drop was inverted, and a naive fix produces two rows where typed dispatch silently takes the command file. | a project `commands/review.md` and a `review` skill |
| ⬜ | 🔴 | `PLG-N9` | **The app tells you a plugin update exists, and updating is honest about what it did not do.** With an installed plugin whose marketplace catalog carries a newer version, open the plugin's detail page: the badge must show the available version, and the Actions menu must offer Update. Run it — the success notice must tell you to refresh the plugin runtime, because the running session keeps the previous version's hooks and MCP servers until you do. Then confirm enable, disable and uninstall raise NO such notice. A silent "updated" toast is the failure. | an installed plugin plus a marketplace catalog advertising a newer version of it |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `PLG-N10` | **No badge for a dependency-held plugin.** A plugin whose catalog latest falls OUTSIDE the range an installed dependent pins must show no update badge and no Update action — a control that either does nothing or breaks the dependent. | a held plugin plus a dependent pinning a range the catalog latest violates |

## What has burned us before

Seven traps, each of which has already cost a real measurement or produced a
false result. They are short on purpose — this is the part a driver re-reads
before a wave.

**0. Never run a wave while anything edits renderer source.** `electron:dev`
serves the renderer from Vite; an executor's edit to any imported renderer file
triggers an HMR full reload MID-CASE, and the reload's splash overlay
(`fixed inset-0 z-splash`) then eats every coordinate click while state reads
keep working — so the case reports "clicked, nothing happened" against a
healthy product. Cost a real measurement on 2026-07-31: AGT-2's dismiss check
FAILED against a dismiss that works (proven by DOM click on the same row while
`elementFromPoint` at the button's centre returned the splash div). The wave
and a renderer-editing executor are mutually exclusive — schedule them apart,
and if a case's clicks start missing halfway through, check for the splash
before filing anything.

**1. Type with real keys; `Input.insertText` fires none.** It commits the whole
string at once, so the slash palette never opens (`useInlineSlashCommand` matches
a slash token *before the caret*) and no arrow/Enter can select a row. A run that
used it filed AGT-1 as PASS and the pass had to be retracted. The harness now
refuses the method outright.

**2. A click must land inside the visible element.** One run clicked a palette
row at y=1006 in a 900px viewport — outside, so the click closed the palette.
Worse and still open: `AGT-3`'s Allow click hits `InputContainer`'s **hidden
measuring copy**, whose box is real (`810x226`) so the guard does not refuse it,
while the visible card is clamped to `200`. Select the **visible** element, not
the first match.

**3. Rebuild before you measure, and know which bundle you rebuilt.** `electron:dev`
runs both the host and the Pi subprocess from **built** bundles. A renderer change
is picked up by Vite immediately; a **main-process** change is not — the running
instance keeps the old bundle in memory, so a fix can be present on disk and
absent in the app. Confirm the code under test is in the bundle, e.g.
`grep -c <symbol> apps/electron/dist/main.cjs`.

**4. A finding that is an ABSENCE needs a positive control.** "X does not happen"
is worth nothing until something on the same rig makes X happen. The prompt-cache
row was refuted this way: memory edits moved no cache number, and the result was
only trustworthy because an output-style change on the same rig moved it
36416 → 16384.

**5. Read the state, not the pixels — but check the state can see what you ask.**
`security.pendingPermissions` filters by **exact session id**, and a fork's
request carries the **child's** id, so the parent list is `[]` before and after
regardless. A row was filed on that read as if it distinguished a stale card from
a second prompt. It cannot.

**6. Two concurrent `bun test` processes manufacture failures.** Several session
suites mutate `process.env.NESSY_CONFIG_DIR` at module scope. Before recording a
new failure, check nothing else is running — and check the known-failing list at
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-known-failing-tests.md`.

### Hook input payloads (R54)

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `HOK-N5` | **A doc-shaped hook reads the documented fields.** A `FileChanged` hook whose script reads `.file_path` and `.event` must get an ABSOLUTE path and one of `add` / `change` / `unlink` — create a new file (expect `add`) and then edit it (expect `change`). A `CwdChanged` hook must see `new_cwd` and, on the second change, `old_cwd`. A `PreCompact` hook on an automatic compaction must receive `custom_instructions` as an EMPTY STRING, not a missing key; on `/compact do X` it must receive `do X`. Requires a PACKAGED build — `electron:dev` runs the Pi subprocess from a bundle that may predate the change. | probe plugin with `FileChanged`, `CwdChanged` and `PreCompact` command hooks, each writing its stdin JSON to a file |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `HOK-N6` | **`$CLAUDE_EFFORT` reaches both places the page names.** A hook command's script echoing `$CLAUDE_EFFORT` records the turn's active level, and a Bash tool call from the model echoing the same variable records it too. On a model with no effort support the hook payload must carry NO `effort` key at all — an absent effort must never read as `medium`. | probe plugin with a `PreToolUse` command hook writing `$CLAUDE_EFFORT`; one session on an effort-capable model and one on a model without effort support |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `HOK-N4` | **`PostCompact` now carries conversation content — confirm the user can see that.** A `PostCompact` hook receives `compact_summary` holding the generated summary. Run it once with a WEBHOOK action and confirm what leaves the machine: this is the first hook payload carrying conversation text, so the case exists to make the disclosure visible rather than to bless it. Record what the receiving endpoint sees. | a probe automation on `PostCompact` with a webhook action pointed at a local listener; a session long enough to trigger compaction |

### MCP roots & channel notifications (R57)

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MCP-N13` | **A server can ask what it may see, and is told when that changes.** Connect an MCP server that requests `roots`: it receives the workspace root as a `file://` URI. Change the configured roots and confirm the server gets a roots-changed notification without reconnecting. **Deliberate narrowing to check:** a directory added to ONE session with `/add-dir` must NOT appear — the pool is shared per project, so one session's grant must not widen what other sessions' servers see. | any MCP server that logs the roots it is given |
| ⬜ | 🔴 | `MCP-N14` | **A notification reaches the session, and cannot forge its own fence.** With channel push enabled, have the server send a notification while the session is idle: it wakes and the event appears wrapped in a `<channel …>` element. Then send one whose BODY contains a literal `</channel>` and a fake harness marker line — the element must not terminate early and the forged marker must arrive defanged. Several notifications during a turn arrive as one group on the next. | an MCP server you can trigger notifications from, including a hostile body |

### Headless init frame (R59)

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `HL-N1` | **CI can gate on the first frame.** A headless run's first stdout line is a `system`/`init` frame carrying session id, cwd, model, permission mode, tools, plugins and MCP servers. With every plugin healthy, `plugin_errors` and `mcp_server_errors` are ABSENT — not empty arrays. Check with a key test (`'plugin_errors' in frame`), because an empty array passes a falsy check and a gate written that way proves nothing. | a headless run with all plugins loading cleanly |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `HL-N2` | **A demoted plugin is reported, a healthy one is not.** Install a plugin whose user config is unset (a demoting condition) and confirm it appears in `plugin_errors` AND is absent from `plugins`. Then a plugin whose secret lands in an unredacted sink — a NON-demoting condition — must load normally and produce NO entry. A dependency failure must appear exactly once, not twice. | one plugin with required user config unset; one that trips the unredacted-sink diagnostic |

### Subagent limits (R60)

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `SUB-N1` | **The refusal is the documented one, at the documented number.** Ask for more concurrent subagents than the machine allows and read the refusal: it must say `Concurrent subagent limit reached` and name the number in force, NOT `live-agent-ceiling-reached`. Note the number is memory-derived — about 7 on a 16 GiB machine — so record the machine's RAM with the result. Then set `NESSY_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS` lower and confirm the new number is what you hit. | a prompt that fans out to more subagents than the ceiling |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `SUB-N2` | **A `/subtask` fork is never blocked by that limit**, even with the subagent ceiling saturated — the page says so explicitly. | the SUB-N1 fixture, plus a `/subtask` while it is saturated |
| ⬜ | 🔴 | `SUB-N3` | **A child cannot speak in the harness's voice.** Run a subagent whose report begins with a verbatim `[harness: …: none]` line claiming it was checked clean, and then asks for elevated permissions. The parent must show exactly ONE undefanged harness line, it must lead, and it must name the forgery — not the child's `none`. | a subagent instructed to emit that exact first line |

It must refuse with the overrides error. The same skill invoked by the user with `/name` must run. This is the case where "hidden from the listing" and "actually blocked" differ. | one skill plus a settings file setting it to `user-invocable-only` |

A plugin skill named in the overrides map is unaffected. | four skills, one per state, plus one plugin skill |

Record what the user sees. | one scheduled task with the worktree toggle on that writes a file |

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |

| ⬜ | 🟡 | `CLI-N1` | **An unknown flag stops the run before anything happens.** `run "..." --allowedTools Bash` exits 1 with `Unknown flag` on stderr, and NO server is spawned, no session created, no message sent. This is the widest behaviour change in the unit — every command now rejects an unrecognised dash-token. | — |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `CLI-N2` | **Signals produce the documented codes.** SIGINT → 130, SIGTERM → 143, and in both cases cleanup runs BEFORE the exit (the spawned server is torn down). Verify on a packaged build, not `bun run`. | a long-running headless turn to interrupt |
| ⬜ |  | `CLI-N3` | **Stdin is capped and says so.** Pipe more than 10 MiB into a headless run: it exits non-zero with a clear error rather than hanging or truncating silently. ### Memory imports (R55)

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MEM-N1` | **An `@path` import actually loads.** Put `See @notes/api.md for the API and @notes/style.md for conventions.` mid-sentence in the project MEMORY.md, then ask the model to quote a line that exists ONLY in `notes/api.md`. It must be able to. Before R55 the host deleted that line and Pi kept it as prose, so the content never arrived either way. Then wrap one as `` `@notes/api.md` `` and put another inside a ```fence``` and confirm neither loads (memory.md:99). **Note the file family:** this is MEMORY.md — the same test in a CLAUDE.md is not expected to pass yet (tracked as R77). | a project MEMORY.md with two topic files beside it |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MEM-N2` | **Both deliveries agree, and nothing is double-shipped.** With the same fixture, check `/context` and the model's own view: an imported file's content must appear ONCE, not twice, and the count the context readout reports for memory files must match what the model can actually quote. A body that appears twice means the import was spliced AND shipped as its own layer; a body the model cannot quote but `/context` counts means the two deliveries disagreed again. | the MEM-N1 fixture, plus `/context` |

### Memory write feedback (R56)

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MEM-N3` | **The model is told when the index is full.** Grow MEMORY.md to just under 200 lines, then have the model save one more memory: the save succeeds and the model receives a reminder to shorten it. Push it past 200 lines and save again: **the write still succeeds** (check the file on disk) and the model receives an ERROR telling it to rewrite the index — text that includes "The write succeeded", because on the Codex path the error flag itself is invisible. A silent success here is the failure: everything past the limit is dropped on the next load, so the model would be writing into a void. | a project MEMORY.md padded to ~199 lines |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MEM-N4` | **The write stamp does not cost a cache flush.** With a MEMORY.md that HAS YAML frontmatter, save two memories in a row and confirm the file gains a `modified:` ISO timestamp that changes each time — and that the model's pinned memory block does NOT contain it (ask the model to quote the start of its memory block, or read the prompt dump). A file WITHOUT frontmatter must not gain any. If the timestamp reaches the pinned block, every memory write invalidates the prompt cache from that point on. | one MEMORY.md with frontmatter, one without |

### Marketplace auto-update & suggestions (R63)

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MKT-N1` | **A managed `autoUpdate` reaches the marketplace, and a typo does not remove it.** Deliver a managed policy with `"extraKnownMarketplaces": {"corp": {"source": …, "autoUpdate": true}}` and confirm the marketplace's auto-update toggle reads ON and is not user-editable (managed). Then deliver the same policy with `"autoUpdate": "yes"` and confirm the marketplace is STILL DELIVERED with auto-update off, and that config diagnostics carry a receipt naming the field. A missing marketplace here is the failure: an optional modifier must never take the whole delivery away. | a managed policy file with one `extraKnownMarketplaces` entry, edited between runs |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MKT-N2` | **Auto-update runs after startup, not during the turn you are in.** With auto-update enabled on a marketplace whose upstream has moved, start a session and confirm nothing changes under you; within ten minutes the marketplace catalog refreshes and a notification points at Plugins → Refresh runtime. Set `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER` and confirm nothing runs at all; then add `FORCE_AUTOUPDATE_PLUGINS=1` and confirm it runs again. **Known gap:** only the marketplace CATALOG refreshes — installed plugin bundles are not updated on disk yet, so do not score that as a pass. | a git marketplace you can push to between app launches |
| ⬜ | 🔴 | `MKT-N3` | **A suggestion needs the name AND the source — this is the security case.** With `pluginSuggestionMarketplaces: ["corp-plugins"]` in managed settings and a matching `extraKnownMarketplaces` entry, a plugin declaring `relevance.cwd` for the open project is pinned at the top of Discover with a "suggested for this directory" badge. Now register a DIFFERENT marketplace under the same allowlisted name (different source) and confirm NOTHING is pinned. A suggestion surfacing there means an administrator's allowlist can be captured by whoever registers the name first. | managed settings with the allowlist; two marketplaces, one legitimate and one squatting the name |

### Model selection defaults (R52)

| | | case | what it proves | fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MDL-N1` | **Effort defaults per model, and the change is visible.** On a Claude-runtime session, open a fresh session with no effort ever chosen and read the effort the turn actually ran at: it must be `high` (`xhigh` on Opus 4.7), not `medium` (`model-config.md:453`). On a Nessy Gateway session the same check must still read `medium` — the gateway models are not in the table, and a change there would mean the fallback stopped working. This is a SPEND change on Claude sessions, so read the level rather than assuming it. | one Claude-runtime connection and one gateway connection; a config with no `defaultThinkingLevel` |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MDL-N2` | **The hold, including its upgrade edge.** With a stored `defaultThinkingLevel` of `low` set before this build, open a first session on Fable 5 / Opus 4.8 / Opus 4.7: the model's default must OVERRIDE the stored level and survive an app restart (`model-config.md:455`). Then choose a level explicitly — the hold clears and the chosen level carries into the next session and to other models. On Opus 5 the previously set level must carry over untouched: it has a default and no hold, and treating the two lists as one is the failure to catch. | a config written by an earlier build: `defaultThinkingLevel: "low"` and NO explicit-choice marker |
| ⬜ | 🟡 | `MDL-N3` | **The fallback chain walks and then lets go.** Configure `fallbackModel` as three entries where the first two are unavailable and the third works, then send a turn: the turn must succeed on the third, showing one switch notice per hop (`model-config.md:364`). Send a second message and confirm it starts on the PRIMARY again — the switch is turn-scoped, and a session that stays on the fallback is the old behaviour. Then set four entries with a duplicate and confirm only three distinct models are ever tried, deduped before the cap. Reachable only via `--fallback-model a,b,c` or `NESSY_FALLBACK_MODEL` — the setting itself has no UI and `syncConfigDefaults` overwrites the user copy on every launch. | a connection with three model ids, two of them bogus so they fail fast; run the CLI with `--fallback-model` |

### Cycle 2026-07-31 verdicts (post V1/V2/C/S/W/A, fresh bundles)

- **AGT-1 ✅ 10/10** — including the once-void negative control: the model did
  not leak the codeword into the subagent input, and the named subagent could
  not answer it.
- **AGT-2 ✅ 14/14** — after three fixes this cycle: the children-domain
  invalidation on stop (W1), the dismissed-set surviving reloads, and dismiss
  deciding from the freshest snapshot rather than a stale row prop.
- **AGT-3 ✅ 7/7** — three passes across two days.
- **AGT-4 ⚠️** — both style halves PASS live (fork styled, named subagent not —
  after W2 fixed the DRIVER to read the child's own transcript; the product
  guard shipped 29a70d6b). Remaining: the rewind-file assertion is not yet
  driven by the case, and the memory half needs NESSY_CDP_MEMORY_MARKER — case
  debt, not product. The row is now split into `AGT-4a`/`AGT-4b`/`AGT-4c` so a
  red verdict names which of the three broke.
- **AGT-5 ⚠️** — all five checks pass; both spawns COMPLETE after W3 (the
  worktree-project-required refusal is gone). The earlier note that "no field
  carries the effort a spawn actually used" was true only of the runtime
  snapshot: the CHILD SESSION's own `thinkingLevel` carries it and is readable
  through `getSessions()`, so the case is blocked by a DRIVER gap, not by
  design — see the rewritten row.
- **AGT-6 ⚠️** — the UserPromptSubmit half PASSES live (codeword phrasing). The
  SessionStart(compact) halves are blocked economically: compaction only has
  work once history exceeds the Pi SDK's keepRecentTokens tail, and buying
  ~20k gateway tokens per regression run is not a fixture. Recipe: run against
  a disposable session that already carries a long history.
- **AGT-7 🔒** — fixture recipe below stands (install the probe plugin, provide
  values, pass the env).
- **AGT-8 🔒** — awaits the operator sudo one-liner below; split into
  `AGT-8a`/`AGT-8b` because only the enforcement half is drivable.

### Cycle 2026-08-05 verdicts — `CLR-1…15`, packaged build (T8)

Run on `NessyDesktop-1.17.0-mac-arm64` built from `becd7138` with
`NESSY_FEATURE_CLAUDE_RUNTIME=1` (and a second artifact adding
`NESSY_FEATURE_CODEX_RUNTIME=1` for `CLR-10`/`CLR-13`), against the live gateway.

- **13 ✅ / 2 ⚠️ / 0 ❗.** `CLR-12` is the product finding worth carrying forward:
  the adapter's stall handling is right on the wire (`CLR-11` proves it), but the
  SDK's retry policy turns a hard-stalled upstream into a multi-minute
  `Responding…` rather than a surfaced error. `CLR-10` is an EVIDENCE gap, not a
  product one — see its row.
- **Three checks were nearly banked wrong, and review caught the third.**
  `CLR-13` passed as "the image arrived" until the fixture was moved out of the
  session cwd — the model had read it off disk. `CLR-8` means nothing without
  the gateway's 400-on-a-Haiku-id measurement beside it. And `CLR-15` was first
  written as "the summary agent starts its own adapter" on the strength of a
  loopback port that turned out to be the app's **websocket server** — the run
  that claimed it has zero `adapter listening` lines, while the runs either side
  of it have four and one. A port number is not a role.
- `CLR-10`'s prompt size is a measurement, not a guess, and the first attempt at
  it (335 kB → 1.6 s ttfb) would have banked a pass for a turn that never
  crossed the 2 s heartbeat threshold.
- **The flag this cycle set is now the default.** `NESSY_FEATURE_CLAUDE_RUNTIME=1`
  stopped distinguishing anything on 2026-08-06, so `CLR-7` no longer exercises a
  flag and the untested arm moved to `PRV-N18` (`=0`).

### Cycle 2026-08-13 verdicts — Q12, the combined 1.25.1 gate, STOOD DOWN mid-run

Packaged unsigned `.app` from `12f7e79b3`; the run was stopped by owner decision
after `codex-app-server` moved `rust-v0.142.5` → `rust-v0.147.0`, which replaces
the artifact under test. Evidence: `.superpowers/sdd/q12-combined-evidence.md`.

- **Measured and still valid** (nothing here depends on the codex binary):
  **S1 sandbox-off-by-default ✅** — a real `Bash` turn on Pi with no `sandbox`
  key in `~/.nessy-agent/config.json` produced
  `isolationProfile: "unsandboxed:disabled-by-config"` +
  `isolationDowngrade: {"reason":"sandboxDisabled"}` on the `semanticId: "bash"`
  record AND on two `plugin-hook` records beside it, and the doctor's `Sandbox:`
  section (`config-doctor.ts:486`) independently reports
  `disabled (config default) — …`. Two surfaces, one answer.
- **Started, no verdict:** S2 (headless Chromium) — the `Bash` row was still
  `executing` when the app was stopped.
- **Not started:** S1's positive control (`sandbox.enabled: true`), S3, S5, S6,
  and all of Part A (agents / plugins / MCP / skills on Pi and Claude).
- **Annulled by the version change, NOT gaps:** the whole Codex column of Part A
  and **S4** (`sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` + `approval_policy` unchanged
  in the generated `config.toml`). They must be re-measured on the 0.147 build;
  a 0.142 result would not have carried.
- **New hygiene rule, learnt here — see the two bullets added below.** The real
  `config.json` already held `sandbox.enabled: true` and
  `bypassPermissionsAcknowledged: true` when this run started, left by a previous
  attempt: the exact two values S1's positive control and S5 exist to discover.

Packaged unsigned `.app` from `32118927e`, isolated `NESSY_CONFIG_DIR`. Report:
`.superpowers/sdd/q13-codex-0147-report.md`; evidence:
`.superpowers/sdd/q13-codex-0147-evidence.md`. **This closes the "annulled by the
version change" list above** — the Codex column of Part A and **S4** were both
re-measured on 0.147.

Bundle gate proved the VERSION: the packaged `codex-app-server` hashes to
`manifest.json`'s `binarySha256`, `upstream.tag` is `rust-v0.147.0`, and the
binary self-reports `codex-app-server 0.147.0`.

- **S4 ✅ (re-measured on 0.147)** — read from the LIVE `CODEX_HOME/config.toml`
  during a turn: `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` and
  `approval_policy = "on-request"` **unchanged**. The isolation wave did not
  reach the approval layer, and 0.147 accepted the file.
- **Codex baseline ✅** — session starts in a directory this codex install had
  never seen and completes a turn in ~3 s (`Codex adapter listening …` →
  `text_complete` → `complete`), echoing a run-minted nonce. Repeated on a second
  fresh directory.
- **Codex MCP ✅** — `mcp__sources__plugin_cdp-echo-probe_echo__whoami`
  dispatched, per-call `nonce` confirmed **in the fixture server's own log** and
  carried back into the reply; three turns, three nonces. The transcript row
  exists (`toolStatus: completed`) and renders as `whoami` — the `sources` bridge
  segment does NOT leak into the row. **This supersedes the 2026-08-11 standing
  note that the Codex path records no tool row.**
- **Codex denial ✅** — a real mouse click on Deny took
  `awaitingApprovalCount` 1→0 and produced `toolStatus: "error"` /
  `"user rejected MCP tool call"` with the model told why. Scope: the
  `item/permissions/requestApproval` path; the legacy `execCommandApproval` /
  `applyPatchApproval` whose `ReviewDecision.denied` changed shape never fired,
  so **that fix is still reasoned, not measured**.
- **`#36960` trust gate — no stall, and inert on our path.** The config we write
  carries no `[projects]` table; `codex-app-server` appends
  `trust_level = "trusted"` for the cwd itself. Proven by a 50 ms watcher (FIRST
  snapshot 0 matches, LATEST 1) and by `trust_level` having 0 hits in our source
  and in packaged `main.cjs` against 14 in the codex binary. In the same run
  Nessy's own modal was answered **"Keep restricted"** for that folder — the two
  layers disagree (**Q13-D3**).
- **`#28772` MCP elicitation ❗ FAIL (Q13-D1, sev 2)** — a probe server's
  `elicitation/create` is answered `-32601 Method not found` on Codex because the
  pool client advertises only `roots`. Positive control: the same server on Pi
  got `elicitation: {form,url}` and the request was held open the full 120 s.
  Seam: `setElicitationHandler` has ONE caller, `pi-agent.ts:1053`;
  `codex-agent.ts` and `claude-agent.ts` never wire it, so **the Claude runtime
  is implicated and untested**.
- **`failed to deserialize` — zero on 0.147**, with the `this.debug` channel
  proven live by the adapter line. The log's only hit is dated 2026-08-11 on
  0.142.5 and is the exact defect this wave fixed.
- **Codex agents — deferred gap intact**: `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent`/… exist,
  `SUBAGENTS=NONE`, and `capabilities.getCatalog` returns 0 rows of
  `kind: "agent"` for a Codex session.
- **Open on Codex:** the permission card's header reads `Tool: sources` and calls
  `sources` "the MCP server" (**Q13-D2**, sev 2); a Codex model answers
  `TOOLS=NONE` when asked to enumerate its `mcp__` tools, then calls one
  (**Q13-D4**, sev 3 — consistent with upstream `#29486` putting MCP tools behind
  tool search, and it is what turns three `MCP-W2` checks red without any mint
  regression).

| AGT-4a/c | `~/.nessy-agent/output-styles/cdp-probe.md` — style whose body demands the token on every reply | `NESSY_CDP_OUTPUT_STYLE_ID=custom:cdp-probe NESSY_CDP_OUTPUT_STYLE_MARKER=OSTYLE-CDP-9241`; `NESSY_CDP_MEMORY_MARKER` for the memory half |
| AGT-4b | a fork and a named subagent that each write one distinct marker file before the rewind | — |
| AGT-5 | `~/.nessy-agent/agents/cdp-high-effort.md` (frontmatter `effort: high`) AND `~/.nessy-agent/agents/cdp-inherit-effort.md` (no `effort` key — the reader half is unfalsifiable without it, because a declared level resolves before the session level at both spawns) | `NESSY_CDP_HIGH_EFFORT_SUBAGENT=cdp-high-effort NESSY_CDP_INHERIT_EFFORT_SUBAGENT=cdp-inherit-effort` |
| AGT-6 | `~/.nessy-agent/hooks.json` — UserPromptSubmit echoes marker 1; SessionStart matcher `compact` echoes marker 2 | `NESSY_CDP_PROMPT_HOOK_MARKER=NESSY-CDP-PROMPT-HOOK-77A1 NESSY_CDP_COMPACT_HOOK_MARKER=NESSY-CDP-COMPACT-HOOK-77B2` |
| AGT-7 | built at wave time: author a probe plugin declaring one plain + one sensitive userConfig option, install THROUGH the app, provide values in the dialog | `NESSY_CDP_PLUGIN_OPTION_KEY` + `NESSY_CDP_PLUGIN_SENSITIVE_VALUE` |
| AGT-8a/b | **BLOCKED on admin rights**: `/Library/Application Support/Nessy/` is not writable without sudo. Operator one-liner: `sudo mkdir -p "/Library/Application Support/Nessy" && sudo tee "/Library/Application Support/Nessy/managed-policy.json"` with an `availableModels` allowlist omitting the blocked model; remove after. The same path serves `ADM-N1`…`ADM-N4` | `NESSY_CDP_BLOCKED_MODEL=<a model the policy omits>` |

The fixtures are inert for normal use (a style nobody selects, an agent nobody
invokes, hooks that echo one marker line). **Relaunch the app before the wave**
— V1's mode-enum bundle changes require it regardless.

## Hygiene for a live run

- **Every case creates its own session.** The app auto-deletes an empty unnamed
  session when it leaves the visible set, so name it before the resync click; and
  the sidebar row updates before the content pane does, so wait for an **empty
  composer** before typing — leftover text means you are looking at the previous
  session.
- **The codeword trick leaks.** A prompt that says "remember this exactly" makes
  the model write it into `MEMORY.md`, where a fresh-context subagent then reads
  it. Use a per-run token and scan the memory files.
- **The app is signed into a real corporate gateway.** Never log a token, JWT or
  header value; keep measured runs short — each turn costs money.
- **`~/.nessy/dp_auth_creds.json` is shared with nessy-cli and READ-ONLY to this
  app.** No fixture may write, move or truncate it. A case that needs an expired
  JWT hand-crafts one and pastes it into a connection instead.
- Runs leave named sessions behind; that is fine, but they accumulate.
- **Read the real `config.json` BEFORE you trust a config-shaped verdict, and
  record what was already there.** A previous run of the same gate left
  `sandbox.enabled: true` and `bypassPermissionsAcknowledged: true` sitting in
  `~/.nessy-agent/config.json` — precisely the two values the next run's S1
  positive control and S5 exist to discover. Read either one without noticing and
  you bank two PASSes for something you never caused. This is the stale-process-
  record trap one layer up, and it is worse, because a config key looks
  deliberate. Same for `workspaceRefs`: that attempt also left the operator's own
  workspace with an unresolvable default project, so `createSession` on it throws
  `workspace-default-project-invalid` (`SessionManager.ts:9669`) — create your own
  workspace rather than "fixing" theirs.
- **Build the MCP fixture tree only AFTER the app has opened that config dir
  once.** `fixtures/mcp-naming.ts` writes `config.json` and `mcp.json` into a
  directory the app has never seen, and the storage-readiness gate then blocks
  start-up in `-[NSAlert runModal]` (*"Nessy storage is unavailable"*) with **no
  window, no CDP page target, and no further log line after
  `CLI tools configured:`** — indistinguishable from a hung or sleeping machine,
  and `sample <pid>` is what identifies it. Launch once against an EMPTY config
  dir, let the app create its layout, then lay the fixture files in. Found
  2026-08-13 (Q13); it is a strong candidate for what killed an earlier attempt.
- **Two DOM traps on the permission card.**
  `[data-tutorial="permission-banner"]` returns `innerText === ''` while
  rendering a full-size card — read `textContent`. And an in-page `.click()` on
  `[data-tutorial="permission-allow-button"]` is a **no-op**: 13 attempts over
  two minutes left `awaitingApprovalCount` at 1. Dispatch a real
  `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` at the button's own rect (`lib/viewport.ts`).
- **`~/.nessy-agent/sessions/.pi-processes/` survives restarts and is never
  pruned.** Empty it (or filter every read by a start stamp you wrote down) before
  a run, and check `semanticId` too — `plugin-hook` records outnumber the command
  you actually mean.
- **Back up outside the session scratchpad.** A scratchpad can be recreated under
  you mid-run — one was here, during an ENOSPC event, taking the byte copy of the
  operator's `config.json` and 19 moved process records with it. Keep the byte
  copy next to the original (`config.json.bak-<date>`) and write down the exact
  keys you changed, so a restore is possible without it.
